尼塞韦单抗 免疫后第一年和第二年的结果。

First- and Second-Year Outcomes After Nirsevimab Immunization.
作者： Amélie Gabet, Epiphane Kolla, Ludovic Tréluyer, Marion Bertrand, Marie-Joelle Jabagi, Olivia Weill, Antoine Rachas, Valérie Olié, Mahmoud Zureik
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估 尼塞韦单抗 在连续季节中对 RSV-LRTI 相关住院治疗的有效性,及其与随访第一年和第二年因非 RSV 感染和哮喘住院的关系。
设计与对象
这项 队列研究 研究使用法国国家健康数据系统分析了 2 个全国匹配的 队列研究,其中包括 2023 年和 2024 年免疫接种活动之前在法国本土出生的婴儿。接受 尼塞韦单抗 的婴儿与未免疫婴儿以 1:1 进行匹配。两种 队列研究 均在免疫后随访 1 年,并对 2023 队列研究 进行第二年随访。
核心方法
数据分析于 2023 年 2 月至 2025 年 8 月进行。使用加权 Cox 比例风险模型评估 RSV-LRTI 相关、非 RSV 感染和哮喘住院情况。
主要结果
Among 37 366 infant pairs in 2023 and 87 763 pairs in 2024, RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations during the first year of follow-up occurred in 299 (0.8%) immunized vs 899 (2.4%) unimmunized infants in 2023 and 456 (0.5%) vs 1711 (1.9%), respectively, in 2024. Nirsevimab was associated with a lower rate of RSV-LRTI-related hospitalization in both the 2023 and 2024 cohort during the first year of follow-up, with effectiveness estimates of 66% (95% CI, 61%-70%) and 72% (95% CI, 69%-75%), respectively. An association with higher rates of hospitalization for ear, nose, and throat (ENT) bacterial infections was observed (2023 cohort: weighted hazard ratio [wHR], 1.36; 95% CI, 1.00-1.84; 2024 cohort: wHR, 1.43; 95% CI, 1.15-1.78).
解释与创新
作者得出的结论是,尼塞韦单抗 与 2023-2024 年和 2024-2025 年季节第一年 RSV-LRTI 相关住院治疗的大幅减少有关。然而,他们指出,这与非 RSV 感染或哮喘导致的住院治疗减少无关,也与随访第二年的减少无关。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,尼塞韦单抗 可以在免疫后第一年对 RSV-LRTI 相关住院提供显着保护,这可以为未来婴儿 RSV 预防的 疫苗接种 策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对观测数据和潜在混杂因素的依赖而受到限制,但由于样本量大且使用加权 Cox 比例风险模型,证据充足。

First- and Second-Year Outcomes After Nirsevimab Immunization.

Authors: Amélie Gabet, Epiphane Kolla, Ludovic Tréluyer, Marion Bertrand, Marie-Joelle Jabagi, Olivia Weill, Antoine Rachas, Valérie Olié, Mahmoud Zureik
Original abstract (English)
IMPORTANCE: The consistency of nirsevimab effectiveness across consecutive seasons and its broader clinical protective effects remain to be fully characterized. OBJECTIVE: To assess whether first-season effectiveness of nirsevimab against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV)-lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI)-related hospitalizations is maintained during a new RSV season, whether the effectiveness persists beyond the first season, and whether nirsevimab exposure is associated with hospitalizations for non-RSV infections and asthma during the first and second years of follow-up. DESIGN, SETTING, AND PARTICIPANTS: This cohort study analyzed 2 nationwide matched cohorts using the French National Health Data System, including infants born in metropolitan France before the 2023 and 2024 immunization campaigns. Infants receiving nirsevimab were matched 1:1 to unimmunized infants. Both cohorts were followed up for 1 year after immunization, with an additional second year of follow-up for the 2023 cohort. Data analysis was performed from February 2023 to August 2025. EXPOSURES: Single dose of nirsevimab. MAIN OUTCOMES AND MEASURES: RSV-LRTI-related, non-RSV infection, and asthma hospitalizations were assessed using weighted Cox proportional hazards models. RESULTS: This cohort study included 74 672 infants in 2023 and 175 526 in 2024. At baseline, the mean (SD) age of immunized infants was 4.5 (2.0) months in the 2023 cohort; 19 633 (52.6%) were male and 17 703 (47.4%) were female. The mean (SD) age of the 2024 cohort was 4.8 (2.2) months; 44 863 immunized infants (51.1%) were male and 42 900 (48.9%) were female. Among 37 366 infant pairs in 2023 and 87 763 pairs in 2024, RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations during the first year of follow-up occurred in 299 (0.8%) immunized vs 899 (2.4%) unimmunized infants in 2023 and 456 (0.5%) vs 1711 (1.9%), respectively, in 2024. Nirsevimab was associated with a lower rate of RSV-LRTI-related hospitalization in both the 2023 and 2024 cohort during the first year of follow-up, with effectiveness estimates of 66% (95% CI, 61%-70%) and 72% (95% CI, 69%-75%), respectively. An association with higher rates of hospitalization for ear, nose, and throat (ENT) bacterial infections was observed (2023 cohort: weighted hazard ratio [wHR], 1.36; 95% CI, 1.00-1.84; 2024 cohort: wHR, 1.43; 95% CI, 1.15-1.78). No significant association was observed for other outcomes. During the second year of follow-up, no protective association was observed for RSV-LRTI-related hospitalization (wHR, 1.03; 95% CI, 0.73-1.43). An association with higher rates of hospitalization for ENT (wHR, 1.56; 95% CI, 1.10-2.22) and asthma (wHR, 1.24; 95% CI, 1.05-1.46) was observed. CONCLUSIONS AND RELEVANCE: This study found that nirsevimab was associated with a substantial reduction in RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations during the first year across both the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 seasons. It was not associated with reduced hospitalizations due to non-RSV infections or asthma, nor during the second year of follow-up.

Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the effectiveness of nirsevimab against RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations across consecutive seasons and its association with hospitalizations for non-RSV infections and asthma during the first and second years of follow-up.
Design and population
This cohort study analyzed 2 nationwide matched cohorts using the French National Health Data System, including infants born in metropolitan France before the 2023 and 2024 immunization campaigns. Infants receiving nirsevimab were matched 1:1 to unimmunized infants. Both cohorts were followed up for 1 year after immunization, with an additional second year of follow-up for the 2023 cohort.
Core methods
Data analysis was performed from February 2023 to August 2025. RSV-LRTI-related, non-RSV infection, and asthma hospitalizations were assessed using weighted Cox proportional hazards models.
Main results
Among 37 366 infant pairs in 2023 and 87 763 pairs in 2024, RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations during the first year of follow-up occurred in 299 (0.8%) immunized vs 899 (2.4%) unimmunized infants in 2023 and 456 (0.5%) vs 1711 (1.9%), respectively, in 2024. Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that nirsevimab was associated with a substantial reduction in RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations during the first year across both the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 seasons. However, they noted that it was not associated with reduced hospitalizations due to non-RSV infections or asthma, nor during the second year of follow-up.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that nirsevimab may provide significant protection against RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations in the first year after immunization, which could inform future vaccination strategies for RSV prevention in infants.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on observational data and potential confounding factors, but the evidence is strong due to the large sample size and use of weighted Cox proportional hazards models.

COVID-19 后时期通过多重 PCR 检测到的呼吸道病毒病原体的分子 监测:分布、合并感染和季节性模式。

Molecular Surveillance of Respiratory Viral Pathogens Detected by Multiplex PCR During the Post-COVID-19 Period: Distribution, Co-infections, and Seasonal Patterns.
作者： Fatma Avcioğlu, Elif Soysal, Mustafa Behçet, Oya Kalaycioğlu, Yusuf Afşar, Sare Aybek, Ayça Çisem Harre, Hakan Burak Çelik
摘要中文翻译背景:COVID-19 大流行深刻改变了全球呼吸道病毒的传播动态。然而,描述 COVID-19 后病毒 流行病学 的数据仍然有限。本研究旨在描述 COVID-19 后时期通过多重 PCR 检测到的呼吸道病原体的分布、人口统计学关联、合并感染模式和季节性。方法:在这项回顾性单中心研究中,纳入了 2023 年 2 月至 2024 年 3 月期间向三级甲等大学医院提交呼吸道标本的 750 名患者。使用 BioFire FilmArray Respiratory Panel 2.1 Plus 分析样品。使用皮尔逊卡方或费舍尔精确检验评估分类变量之间的关联。结果:56.3%(422/750)的患者中至少检测到一种病原体。在检测到的 535 种病原体中,98.5% 是病毒病原体。人类鼻病毒/肠道病毒 (30.3%) 是最常见的病毒,主要发生在儿童患者 (43.4%) 中,而 SARS-CoV-2 在成人中最常见 (22.9%)。流感病毒 (17.4%) 和 呼吸道合胞病毒 在冬季表现出明显的优势,而人类鼻病毒/肠道病毒和 SARS-CoV-2 则全年传播。儿童患者的总体阳性率和合并感染率显着较高(p<0.001)。22.3%的阳性病例中发现了双重感染。病毒分布与年龄组、性别、住院状况和季节之间观察到显着相关性(p<0.05)。结论:在后 COVID-19 时代,呼吸道病毒继续主导急性呼吸道感染的病因,具有明显的年龄相关模式和 SARS-CoV-2 的持续传播。基于多重 PCR 的分子 监测 提供了对不断演变的病毒 流行病学 的重要见解,并支持对呼吸道病毒动态的持续监测。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究通过表征多重 PCR 检测到的呼吸道病原体的分布、合并感染和季节性模式,解决了对 COVID-19 后时期呼吸道病毒 流行病学 的有限了解。
设计与对象
这项回顾性单中心研究纳入了 750 名患者,他们的呼吸道标本在 2023 年 2 月至 2024 年 3 月期间提交给三级护理大学医院。
核心方法
使用 BioFire FilmArray Respiratory Panel 2.1 Plus 分析样本,并使用 Pearson 卡方检验或 Fisher 精确检验评估分类变量之间的关联。
主要结果
56.3% (422/750) 的患者至少检测到一种病原体,其中 535 种检测到的病原体中 98.5% 是病毒因子。呼吸道合胞病毒 表现出明显的冬季优势,并且与季节显着相关(p<0.05)。
解释与创新
作者解释说,呼吸道病毒在后 COVID-19 时代继续主导急性呼吸道感染的病因,具有明显的年龄相关模式和 SARS-CoV-2 的持续循环。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果强调了基于多重 PCR 的分子 监测 对于持续监测呼吸道病毒动态和为公共卫生策略提供信息的重要性。
局限与证据强度
单中心回顾性设计和对多重 PCR 的依赖(无验证性检测)限制了普遍性,并可能低估合并感染,需要进行适度的置信度评估。

Molecular Surveillance of Respiratory Viral Pathogens Detected by Multiplex PCR During the Post-COVID-19 Period: Distribution, Co-infections, and Seasonal Patterns.

Authors: Fatma Avcioğlu, Elif Soysal, Mustafa Behçet, Oya Kalaycioğlu, Yusuf Afşar, Sare Aybek, Ayça Çisem Harre, Hakan Burak Çelik
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: The COVID-19 pandemic profoundly altered the circulation dynamics of respiratory viruses worldwide. However, data describing post-COVID-19 viral epidemiology remain limited. This study aimed to characterize the distribution, demographic associations, co-infection patterns, and seasonality of respiratory pathogens detected by multiplex PCR in the post-COVID-19 period. METHODS: In this retrospective single-center study, 750 patients whose respiratory tract specimens were submitted to a tertiary care university hospital between February 2023 and March 2024 were included. Samples were analyzed using the BioFire FilmArray Respiratory Panel 2.1 Plus. Associations between categorical variables were evaluated using Pearson's chi-square or Fisher's exact test. RESULTS: At least one pathogen was detected in 56.3% (422/750) of patients. Of 535 detected pathogens, 98.5% were viral agents. Human rhinovirus/enterovirus (30.3%) was the most frequently identified virus overall and predominated in pediatric patients (43.4%), whereas SARS-CoV-2 was most common among adults (22.9%). Influenza viruses (17.4%) and respiratory syncytial virus demonstrated marked winter predominance, while human rhinovirus/enterovirus and SARS-CoV-2 circulated year-round. Overall positivity and co-infection rates were significantly higher in pediatric patients (p<0.001). Co-infections were detected in 22.3% of positive cases. Significant associations were observed between viral distribution and age group, gender, hospitalization status, and season (p<0.05). CONCLUSIONS: Respiratory viruses continue to dominate the etiology of acute respiratory tract infections in the post-COVID-19 era, with distinct age-related patterns and sustained circulation of SARS-CoV-2. Multiplex PCR-based molecular surveillance provides essential insights into evolving viral epidemiology and supports ongoing monitoring of respiratory virus dynamics.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed the limited understanding of respiratory virus epidemiology in the post-COVID-19 period by characterizing the distribution, co-infections, and seasonal patterns of respiratory pathogens detected by multiplex PCR.
Design and population
This retrospective single-center study included 750 patients whose respiratory tract specimens were submitted to a tertiary care university hospital between February 2023 and March 2024.
Core methods
Samples were analyzed using the BioFire FilmArray Respiratory Panel 2.1 Plus, and associations between categorical variables were evaluated using Pearson's chi-square or Fisher's exact test.
Main results
At least one pathogen was detected in 56.3% (422/750) of patients, with 98.5% of 535 detected pathogens being viral agents. Respiratory syncytial virus demonstrated marked winter predominance and was significantly associated with season (p<0.05).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that respiratory viruses continue to dominate the etiology of acute respiratory tract infections in the post-COVID-19 era, with distinct age-related patterns and sustained circulation of SARS-CoV-2.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the importance of multiplex PCR-based molecular surveillance for ongoing monitoring of respiratory virus dynamics and informing public health strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The single-center retrospective design and reliance on multiplex PCR without confirmatory testing limit generalizability and may underestimate co-infections, warranting a moderate confidence assessment.

反式白藜芦醇通过人体重建表皮中的芳烃受体途径减轻炎症并改善皮肤屏障功能。

Trans-resveratrol alleviates inflammation and improves skin barrier function via aryl hydrocarbon receptor pathway in human reconstructed epidermis.
作者： Irini M. Dijkhoff, Sandeep Keshavan, Regina G. Daré, Alke Petri-Fink, Marc Eeman, Agnès Tessier, Barbara Rothen-Rutishauser, Alke Petri‐Fink, Barbara Rothen‐Rutishauser
摘要中文翻译目的:芳烃受体(AHR)是表皮稳态的调节剂,也是化妆品和皮肤病成分的一个有前途的目标,旨在改善皮肤屏障功能和减少炎症。本研究调查了天然多酚反式白藜芦醇 (RSV)(一种化妆品化合物)在受促炎环境损害的皮肤模型中调节下游 AHR 信号并恢复表皮完整性的潜力。方法:将重建的人表皮 (RhE) 暴露于细胞因子混合物(IL-4、IL-13、TNF-α),以诱导模仿特应性湿疹某些方面的表型,即炎症、氧化应激和屏障破坏,这些特征在敏感和反应性皮肤状况中也很常见。通过使用分子、生化和组织学分析评估炎症介质、氧化应激相关标记物、芳烃受体信号传导和表皮屏障相关蛋白,评估 RSV 的影响。结果:细胞因子暴露诱导了明显的炎症反应、芳烃受体信号传导的激活、氧化应激相关基因的表达以及屏障相关标记物的失调。治疗 与 RSV 通过减少 IL1A mRNA 表达和 IL-1α 分泌来减轻炎症表型,通过下调 CYP1A1 和 CYP1B1 来减弱氧化应激相关的 AHR 靶基因反应,同时维持 AHR 和 HMOX1 表达并调节屏障相关基因表达,特别是通过下调 CASP14 和上调紧密连接蛋白 Occludin (OCLN)。结论:这些发现为人类表皮模型提供了机制证据,表明 RSV 可以对抗炎症、减轻氧化应激并支持表皮屏障稳态,这些作用至少部分是通过调节 AHR 下游信号传导介导的。这支持了白藜芦醇作为化妆品和皮肤病配方中的多功能活性成分的潜力,旨在增强皮肤屏障并镇静敏感或受损的皮肤。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究解决了寻找能够恢复表皮完整性并减少受损皮肤炎症的化妆品和皮肤病制剂的需求。其目的是评估反式白藜芦醇(RSV)是否调节芳烃受体(AHR)信号传导以改善皮肤屏障功能并减轻炎症。
设计与对象
该研究使用暴露于细胞因子混合物(IL-4、IL-13、TNF-α)的重建人类表皮(RhE)模型来模拟炎症和屏障破坏的皮肤状况。使用分子、生化和组织学分析评估 RSV 的影响。
核心方法
在细胞因子诱导的炎症后,用 RSV 处理重建的人类表皮。使用分子、生化和组织学方法测量炎症介质、氧化应激标记物、AHR 信号传导和表皮屏障相关蛋白。
主要结果
细胞因子暴露诱导炎症、AHR 信号激活、氧化应激相关基因表达和屏障标记物失调。RSV 治疗 降低 IL1A mRNA 表达和 IL-1α 分泌,下调 CYP1A1 和 CYP1B1,维持 AHR 和 HMOX1 表达,并通过下调 CASP14 和上调 occludin (OCLN) 来调节屏障基因。
解释与创新
作者将 RSV 的作用解释为对抗炎症、减轻氧化应激以及通过调节 AHR 下游信号传导支持表皮屏障稳态。他们强调了 RSV 作为化妆品和皮肤病学应用的多功能活性成分的潜力。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,RSV 可以开发为配方中的活性成分,旨在增强皮肤屏障并降低受损皮肤状况的敏感性,为其在皮肤病学和化妆品中的应用提供机制基础。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外重建的人类表皮模型,该模型可能无法完全复制体内皮肤反应。由于缺乏临床验证并且依赖单一实验系统,证据强度中等。

Trans-resveratrol alleviates inflammation and improves skin barrier function via aryl hydrocarbon receptor pathway in human reconstructed epidermis.

Authors: Irini M. Dijkhoff, Sandeep Keshavan, Regina G. Daré, Alke Petri-Fink, Marc Eeman, Agnès Tessier, Barbara Rothen-Rutishauser, Alke Petri‐Fink, Barbara Rothen‐Rutishauser
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVE: The aryl hydrocarbon receptor (AHR) is a regulator of epidermal homeostasis and a promising target for cosmetic and dermatological ingredients aimed at improving skin barrier function and reducing inflammation. This study investigated the potential of the natural polyphenol trans-resveratrol (RSV), a compound of cosmetic interest, to modulate downstream AHR signalling and restore epidermal integrity in a model of skin compromised by a pro-inflammatory environment. METHODS: A reconstructed human epidermis (RhE) was exposed to a cytokine cocktail (IL-4, IL-13, TNF-α) to induce a phenotype mimicking some aspects of atopic eczema, namely inflammation, oxidative stress and barrier disruption, features also prevalent in sensitive and reactive skin conditions. The effects of RSV were evaluated by assessing inflammatory mediators, oxidative stress-related markers, aryl hydrocarbon receptor signalling and epidermal barrier-associated proteins using molecular, biochemical and histological analyses. RESULTS: Cytokine exposure induced a pronounced inflammatory response, activation of aryl hydrocarbon receptor signalling, oxidative stress-related gene expression and dysregulation of barrier-related markers. Treatment with RSV attenuated the inflamed phenotype by reducing IL1A mRNA expression and IL-1α secretion, attenuating oxidative stress-associated AHR target gene responses through downregulation of CYP1A1 and CYP1B1, while maintaining AHR and HMOX1 expression and modulating barrier-associated gene expression, particularly by downregulating CASP14 and upregulating the tight junction protein occludin (OCLN). CONCLUSION: These findings provide mechanistic evidence in a human epidermis model that RSV can counteract inflammation, mitigate oxidative stress and support epidermal barrier homeostasis, actions mediated, at least in part, through modulation of AHR downstream signalling. This supports the potential of resveratrol as a multifunctional active ingredient in cosmetic and dermatological formulations designed to strengthen the skin barrier and calm sensitive or compromised skin.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed the need to identify cosmetic and dermatological agents that can restore epidermal integrity and reduce inflammation in compromised skin. It aimed to evaluate whether trans-resveratrol (RSV) modulates aryl hydrocarbon receptor (AHR) signalling to improve skin barrier function and mitigate inflammation.
Design and population
The study used a reconstructed human epidermis (RhE) model exposed to a cytokine cocktail (IL-4, IL-13, TNF-α) to mimic inflammatory and barrier-disrupted skin conditions. The effects of RSV were assessed using molecular, biochemical, and histological analyses.
Core methods
A reconstructed human epidermis was treated with RSV following cytokine-induced inflammation. Inflammatory mediators, oxidative stress markers, AHR signalling, and epidermal barrier-associated proteins were measured using molecular, biochemical, and histological methods.
Main results
Cytokine exposure induced inflammation, AHR signalling activation, oxidative stress-related gene expression, and dysregulation of barrier markers. RSV treatment reduced IL1A mRNA expression and IL-1α secretion, downregulated CYP1A1 and CYP1B1, maintained AHR and HMOX1 expression, and modulated barrier genes by downregulating CASP14 and upregulating occludin (OCLN).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted RSV’s effects as counteracting inflammation, mitigating oxidative stress, and supporting epidermal barrier homeostasis through modulation of AHR downstream signalling. They highlighted RSV’s potential as a multifunctional active ingredient for cosmetic and dermatological applications.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest RSV could be developed as an active ingredient in formulations designed to strengthen the skin barrier and reduce sensitivity in compromised skin conditions, offering a mechanistic basis for its use in dermatology and cosmetics.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was limited to an in vitro reconstructed human epidermis model, which may not fully replicate in vivo skin responses. The evidence strength is moderate due to the absence of clinical validation and reliance on a single experimental system.

RSV下呼吸道感染的临床特征及预后不良的危险因素:一项两中心研究

Clinical characteristics and risk factors for poor prognosis of RSV lower respiratory tract infection: a two-center study
作者： Tianyu Chen, Xiaolei Guo, Wenyin Tang, Feng Liu, Jiahua Jv, Hang Sun
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
RSV 是五岁以下儿童急性下呼吸道感染 (LRTI) 的主要原因,因此需要简单的临床指标来预测 RSV-LRTI 的不良预后。
设计与对象
这项回顾性 队列研究 研究纳入了 2023 年 4 月至 2025 年 6 月期间在中国两家医院收治的 RSV-LRTI 儿童,从患者记录中收集人口统计和临床数据。
核心方法
根据不良预后事件(包括入住 ICU、住院时间延长和严重肺炎)对患者进行分层,并使用 Firth 回归模型评估相关性。
主要结果
Among 257 children, 254 (98.8%) had cough, 144 (56.0%) wheezing, and 194 (75.4%) febrile symptoms; higher respiratory rate at admission was independently associated with ICU admission (OR 1.132, P < 0.05), the presence of three concave sign with prolonged hospital stay (OR 5.242, 95% CI 1.360–19.951, P = 0.017), and procalcitonin with severe pneumonia (OR 1.616, 95% CI 1.074–2.837, P = 0.020).
解释与创新
作者得出的结论是,呼吸频率、三凹征和降钙素原等简单的临床指标可以促进早期识别存在 RSV-LRTI 不良后果风险的儿童。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可能为儿科 RSV-LRTI 的早期风险分层和有针对性的干预措施提供信息,以减少严重后果并优化资源利用。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其回顾性设计、ICU病例数量少和单季数据而受到限制,需要谨慎解释和前瞻性验证。

Clinical characteristics and risk factors for poor prognosis of RSV lower respiratory tract infection: a two-center study

Authors: Tianyu Chen, Xiaolei Guo, Wenyin Tang, Feng Liu, Jiahua Jv, Hang Sun
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Objective Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is a leading cause of acute lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) in children under five years of age. In this study, we investigated the value of simple and readily available clinical indicators in predicting poor prognosis in children with RSV-LRTI. Methods This was a retrospective cohort study of children with RSV-LRTI admitted to Children’s Hospital of Nanjing Medical University (April 2023–May 2024) and Gaoyou People’s Hospital (December 2024–June 2025). Demographic and clinical data were collected from patient records, and patients were stratified based on three adverse prognostic events: admission to Intensive Care Unit (ICU), prolonged hospital stay, and severe pneumonia. Results A total of 257 eligible children were enrolled, of whom 254 (98.8%) exhibited cough, 144 (56.0%) presented with wheezing and 194 (75.4%) developed febrile symptoms. 220 (85.6%) cases of RSV-LRTI occurred in winter and spring, with a higher proportion of cases occurring in infants under 1 year of age. A higher respiratory rate at admission was independently associated with ICU admission (Firth regression: OR 1.132, both P < 0.05), though this finding was limited by the small number of ICU cases. The presence of three concave sign was independently associated with prolonged hospital stay (OR 5.242, 95% CI 1.360-19.951, P = 0.017). Procalcitonin (PCT) was an independent risk factor for severe pneumonia (OR 1.616, 95% CI 1.074–2.837, P = 0.020). Conclusion Higher respiratory rate at admission was independently associated with ICU admission, and the presence of three concave sign was independently associated with prolonged hospital stay. PCT was an independent risk factor for severe pneumonia. These simple clinical indicators facilitate early identification of children at risk of adverse outcomes.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
RSV is a leading cause of acute lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) in children under five years of age, creating a need for simple clinical indicators to predict poor prognosis in RSV-LRTI.
Design and population
This retrospective cohort study enrolled children with RSV-LRTI admitted to two hospitals in China between April 2023 and June 2025, collecting demographic and clinical data from patient records.
Core methods
Patients were stratified based on adverse prognostic events including ICU admission, prolonged hospital stay, and severe pneumonia, and associations were assessed using Firth regression models.
Main results
Among 257 children, 254 (98.8%) had cough, 144 (56.0%) wheezing, and 194 (75.4%) febrile symptoms; higher respiratory rate at admission was independently associated with ICU admission (OR 1.132, P < 0.05), the presence of three concave sign with prolonged hospital stay (OR 5.242, 95% CI 1.360–19.951, P = 0.017), and procalcitonin with severe pneumonia (OR 1.616, 95% CI 1.074–2.837, P = 0.020).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors conclude that simple clinical indicators such as respiratory rate, three concave sign, and procalcitonin can facilitate early identification of children at risk of adverse outcomes in RSV-LRTI.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings may inform early risk stratification and targeted interventions in pediatric RSV-LRTI to reduce severe outcomes and optimize resource use.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective design, small number of ICU cases, and single-season data, warranting cautious interpretation and prospective validation.

评估 呼吸道合胞病毒 疫苗接种 在风险分层老年人中的成本效益。

Evaluating the cost-effectiveness of respiratory syncytial virus vaccination among risk-stratified older adults.
作者： David W. Hutton, Lisa A. Prosser, Ismael R. Ortega-Sanchez, Andrew J. Leidner, Fiona P. Havers, Mila M. Prill, Michael Whitaker, Lauren E. Roper, Jamison Pike, Michael Melgar
摘要中文翻译呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 对患有慢性病的老年人影响尤为严重。RSV 疫苗 有效但成本高昂,并且许可后研究表明吉兰-巴利综合征 (GBS) 的风险增加。我们通过在年龄≥75岁的成年人和患有与严重RSV疾病风险增加相关的慢性病的50-74岁成年人中模拟RSV 疫苗接种,评估了RSV 疫苗接种的成本效益和风险收益权衡。数据来自 疫苗 试验、同行评审文献、观察性 疫苗 有效性 (VE) 研究和 监测 网络(例如 RSV-NET)。假定 疫苗 保护持续 2 到 3 年。我们估计,与 疫苗 相关的 GBS 相比,RSV 疫苗接种 可以避免更多来自 RSV 的质量调整生命年 (QALY)。社会成本效益因年龄、风险状况和 VE 持续时间而有很大差异。年龄≥75岁成年人的RSV 疫苗每个质量调整生命年(QALY)费用为45,769美元(95%不确定区间,UI:25,505-84,871美元),蛋白质亚基疫苗的有效期为3年;mRNA 疫苗 的 QALY 为 100,571 美元(UI:58,277 美元-225,885 美元)/QALY,有效期为 2 年;mRNA 疫苗 的价格为 70,731 美元(UI:42,069 美元至 159,451 美元),有效期为 3 年。对于患有至少一种慢性病的 60-74 岁成年人来说,疫苗接种 的蛋白质亚基 疫苗 的费用为 21,721 美元(UI:节省成本 - 120,989 美元)/QALY,效果为 3 年;mRNA 疫苗 的 QALY 为 85,781 美元(UI:3368-409,467 美元)/QALY,有效期为 2 年;mRNA 疫苗 的 QALY 为 50,864 美元(UI:3668-249,938 美元),有效期为 3 年。患有至少一种慢性病的 50-59 岁成年人的 疫苗接种 蛋白亚基 疫苗 的费用为 43,070 美元(UI:节省成本 - 266,769 美元)/QALY,有效期为 3 年;mRNA 疫苗 的 QALY 为 152,293 美元(UI:1154-811,138 美元),有效期为 2 年;mRNA 疫苗 的效果为 95,182 美元(UI:节省成本 - 682,508 美元),有效期为 3 年。无论使用何种 疫苗,60-74 岁无慢性病成年人的 疫苗 都具有较高的增量成本效益比(287,793 美元至 503,328 美元/QALY)。如果RSV疾病具有较高的发病率、成本,或死亡率、疫苗接种将更具成本效益。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该评价评估了 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 疫苗接种 在风险分层老年人中的成本效益,特别是年龄≥75岁的成年人和患有与严重 RSV 疾病风险增加相关的慢性病的 50-74 岁成年人。
证据基础与方法
证据基础包括 疫苗 试验、同行评审文献、观察性 疫苗 有效性 (VE) 研究和 监测 网络(例如 RSV-NET)。该审查使用模拟模型来估计成本效益和风险收益权衡。
共识与结论
原始证据未报告
争议与缺口
RSV 疫苗接种 的成本效益根据年龄、风险状况和 VE 持续时间而有很大差异。审查指出,疫苗 的保护被假定持续 2 至 3 年,RSV 疫苗 的长期有效性和安全性存在不确定性。
科研与实践启示
审查表明,如果 RSV 疾病具有更高的 发病率、成本,或 死亡率、疫苗接种 将更具成本效益。它还强调需要进一步研究 RSV 疫苗 的长期有效性和安全性,特别是在不同年龄组和具有不同风险状况的人群中。

Evaluating the cost-effectiveness of respiratory syncytial virus vaccination among risk-stratified older adults.

Authors: David W. Hutton, Lisa A. Prosser, Ismael R. Ortega-Sanchez, Andrew J. Leidner, Fiona P. Havers, Mila M. Prill, Michael Whitaker, Lauren E. Roper, Jamison Pike, Michael Melgar
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) disproportionately affects older adults with chronic conditions. RSV vaccines are effective but costly, and post-licensure studies suggest increased risk of Guillain-Barre syndrome (GBS). We evaluated cost-effectiveness and risk-benefit trade-offs of RSV vaccination by simulating RSV vaccination in adults aged ≥75 years and in adults aged 50-74 years with chronic conditions associated with increased risk of severe RSV disease. Data came from vaccine trials, peer-reviewed literature, observational vaccine effectiveness (VE) studies, and surveillance networks such as RSV-NET. Vaccine protection was assumed to last between 2 and 3 years. We estimated RSV vaccination averts more quality-adjusted life-years (QALYs) from RSV than vaccine-associated GBS. Societal cost-effectiveness varies substantially depending on age, risk condition, and duration of VE. RSV vaccination of adults aged ≥75 years costs $45,769 (95% uncertainty interval, UI: $25,505-$84,871) per quality-adjusted life-year (QALY) for protein subunit vaccines with 3 years of effectiveness; $100,571 (UI: $58,277-$225,885)/QALY for mRNA vaccine with 2 years of effectiveness; and $70,731 (UI: $42,069-$159,451) for mRNA vaccine with 3 years of effectiveness. Vaccination of adults aged 60-74 years with at least one chronic condition costs $21,721 (UI: cost-saving-$120,989)/QALY for protein subunit vaccines with 3 years effectiveness; $85,781 (UI: $3368-$409,467)/QALY for mRNA vaccine with 2 years of effectiveness; and $50,864 (UI: $3668-$249,938)/QALY for mRNA vaccine with 3 years of effectiveness. Vaccination of adults aged 50-59 years with at least one chronic condition costs $43,070 (UI: cost-saving-$266,769)/QALY for protein subunit vaccines with 3 years of effectiveness; $152,293 (UI: $1154-$811,138)/QALY for mRNA vaccine with 2 years of effectiveness; and $95,182 (UI: cost-saving-$682,508) for mRNA vaccine with 3 years of effectiveness. Regardless of the vaccine used, vaccination of adults aged 60-74 years without chronic conditions has high incremental cost effectiveness ratios ($287,793 to $503,328/QALY). If RSV disease had higher incidence, costs, or mortality, vaccination would be more cost-effective.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review evaluates the cost-effectiveness of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccination among risk-stratified older adults, specifically adults aged ≥75 years and adults aged 50-74 years with chronic conditions associated with increased risk of severe RSV disease.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base includes vaccine trials, peer-reviewed literature, observational vaccine effectiveness (VE) studies, and surveillance networks such as RSV-NET. The review uses simulation models to estimate cost-effectiveness and risk-benefit trade-offs.
Consensus and conclusions
RSV vaccines are effective but costly, and post-licensure studies suggest an increased risk of Guillain-Barre syndrome (GBS). The review concludes that RSV vaccination averts more quality-adjusted life-years (QALYs) from RSV than vaccine-associated GBS.
Controversies and gaps
The cost-effectiveness of RSV vaccination varies substantially depending on age, risk condition, and duration of VE. The review notes that vaccine protection was assumed to last between 2 and 3 years, and there is uncertainty about the long-term effectiveness and safety of RSV vaccines.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that if RSV disease had higher incidence, costs, or mortality, vaccination would be more cost-effective. It also highlights the need for further research on the long-term effectiveness and safety of RSV vaccines, particularly in different age groups and populations with varying risk profiles.

全面了解 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV):从 发病机制 到预防。

A comprehensive look at Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV): from pathogenesis to prevention.
作者： Omid Salahi Ardekani, Parisa Hasannezhad, Yasaman Goudarzi, Arash Letafati, Zahra Taghiabadi, Mahshid Bahari, Negar Nayerain Jazi, Ali Qaraee Najafabadi, Somayeh Shatizadeh Malekshahi
摘要中文翻译呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是一种可怕的呼吸道病原体,尤其影响婴儿和儿童。RSV 具有不同的临床严重程度,最常引起轻微的上呼吸道症状,但有时会进展为严重且可能致命的下呼吸道疾病。这种病毒对全球公共卫生产生重大影响,并对医疗保健系统造成巨大压力。RSV 表现出明显的季节性趋势,这些趋势因地理位置和气候带而异,认识到这些模式及其决定因素对于政策制定者有效安排 RSV 免疫计划至关重要。人们越来越关注 RSV 疫苗 不断变化的情况,特别是它们预防感染和降低疾病严重程度的潜力,特别是在弱势群体中。到目前为止,三种 RSV 疫苗:Arexvy、Abrysvo 和 mRESVIA 已获得 FDA 批准。此外,尼塞韦单抗 和 Clesrovimab 等单克隆抗体可预防严重 RSV 疾病,推荐用于婴儿。鉴于 RSV 的重大全球影响,这篇叙述性综述讨论了其多方面的问题,包括 流行病学 和全球影响、临床表现、治疗策略、疫苗 的开发及其作为溶瘤剂的新兴潜力。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是一种可怕的呼吸道病原体,尤其影响婴儿和儿童。RSV 具有不同的临床严重程度,最常引起轻微的上呼吸道症状,但有时会进展为严重且可能致命的下呼吸道疾病。这种病毒对全球公共卫生产生重大影响,并对医疗保健系统造成巨大压力。RSV 表现出明显的季节性趋势,这些趋势因地理位置和气候带而异,认识到这些模式及其决定因素对于政策制定者有效安排 RSV 免疫计划至关重要。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 RSV 现有文献的叙述性综合,重点关注其 流行病学、临床表现、治疗策略、疫苗 的开发以及作为溶瘤剂的潜力。尽管所提供的文本中没有明确详细说明具体的数据库、检索日期和资格标准,但证据来自一系列研究和报告。
共识与结论
RSV 疫苗 已开发并获得 FDA 批准,其中 Arexvy、Abrysvo 和 mRESVIA 是著名的例子。尼塞韦单抗 和 Clesrovimab 等单克隆抗体也用于预防婴儿严重 RSV 疾病。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了对 RSV 疫苗 进行进一步研究的必要性,特别是关于它们在易感人群中预防感染和减轻疾病严重程度的潜力。还需要更好地了解 RSV 的季节性趋势及其决定因素,以改进免疫接种计划。
科研与实践启示
显然需要继续研究和开发 RSV 疫苗 和单克隆抗体,以解决 RSV 对全球公共卫生的影响。此外,还需要改进 监测 并了解 RSV 的季节性模式,以优化免疫计划。

A comprehensive look at Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV): from pathogenesis to prevention.

Authors: Omid Salahi Ardekani, Parisa Hasannezhad, Yasaman Goudarzi, Arash Letafati, Zahra Taghiabadi, Mahshid Bahari, Negar Nayerain Jazi, Ali Qaraee Najafabadi, Somayeh Shatizadeh Malekshahi
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) is a formidable respiratory pathogen that particularly affects infants and children. RSV presents with diverse clinical severity, most often causing mild upper airway symptoms but sometimes progressing to severe and potentially fatal lower respiratory tract disease. This virus has a major global public health impact and places a substantial strain on healthcare systems. RSV exhibits distinct seasonal trends that vary across geographic locations and climate zones, and recognizing these patterns and their determinants is essential for policymakers to schedule RSV immunization programs effectively. Increasing attention has been directed toward the evolving landscape of RSV vaccines, particularly regarding their potential to prevent infection and reduce disease severity, especially among vulnerable populations. Until now, three RSV vaccines, Arexvy, Abrysvo, and mRESVIA, have received approval from the FDA. In addition, monoclonal antibodies such as Nirsevimab and Clesrovimab prevent severe RSV disease and are recommended for infants. Given the significant global impact of RSV, this narrative review addresses its multifaceted aspects, including epidemiology and global impact, clinical manifestations, therapeutic strategies, vaccine development, and its emerging potential as an oncolytic agent.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) is a formidable respiratory pathogen that particularly affects infants and children. RSV presents with diverse clinical severity, most often causing mild upper airway symptoms but sometimes progressing to severe and potentially fatal lower respiratory tract disease. This virus has a major global public health impact and places a substantial strain on healthcare systems. RSV exhibits distinct seasonal trends that vary across geographic locations and climate zones, and recognizing these patterns and their determinants is essential for policymakers to schedule RSV immunization programs effectively.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing literature on RSV, focusing on its epidemiology, clinical manifestations, therapeutic strategies, vaccine development, and potential as an oncolytic agent. The evidence is derived from a range of studies and reports, though the specific databases, search dates, and eligibility criteria are not explicitly detailed in the provided text.
Consensus and conclusions
RSV vaccines have been developed and approved by the FDA, with Arexvy, Abrysvo, and mRESVIA being notable examples. Monoclonal antibodies such as Nirsevimab and Clesrovimab are also used to prevent severe RSV disease in infants.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the need for further research on RSV vaccines, particularly regarding their potential to prevent infection and reduce disease severity in vulnerable populations. There is also a need to better understand the seasonal trends of RSV and their determinants to improve immunization scheduling.
Research and practice implications
There is a clear need for continued research and development of RSV vaccines and monoclonal antibodies to address the global public health impact of RSV. Additionally, there is a need for improved surveillance and understanding of RSV's seasonal patterns to optimize immunization programs.

营养性芳烃受体配体对 呼吸道合胞病毒 感染的治疗潜力:小综述

Therapeutic potential of nutritional aryl hydrocarbon receptor ligands for respiratory syncytial virus infection: a mini review
作者： Sydney E. Green, Antonella Casola, Aline Haas de Mello
摘要中文翻译呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 感染仍然是幼儿和老年人住院的主要原因。尽管最近取得了重大进展,但预防策略仍然存在重大差距,特别是对于年龄较大的婴儿和服务不足的人群,而且迄今为止还没有具体的 治疗 可用。芳烃受体 (AHR) 是一种转录因子,最初被认为是环境污染物毒性作用的介体,后来发现它也可以被食物、肠道微生物组和 宿主 代谢中的配体激活。这些天然存在的或营养性配体激活的 AHR 与有益的生理过程相关。值得注意的是,AHR 在肺部等屏障器官中具有关键功能,包括调节 免疫应答 对感染的影响。AHR 激活是许多病毒逃避 抗病毒药物 免疫的常见策略,但在最近发表的一项研究中,我们发现在 RSV 感染期间,AHR 通路下调并发挥保护作用,并且给 RSV 感染小鼠施用膳食 AHR 前配体吲哚-3-甲醇 (I3C) 会产生有益效果。在这里,我们回顾并讨论了一些研究,这些研究表明营养性 AHR 配体,特别是源自十字花科蔬菜或色氨酸代谢过程中产生的化合物,可能通过积极调节炎症和减少过度的 免疫应答、氧化应激和病毒复制,为 RSV 疾病提供治疗益处。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 感染仍然是幼儿和老年人住院的主要原因。在这里,我们回顾并讨论了一些研究,这些研究表明营养性 AHR 配体,特别是源自十字花科蔬菜或色氨酸代谢过程中产生的化合物,可能通过积极调节炎症和减少过度的 免疫应答、氧化应激和病毒复制,为 RSV 疾病提供治疗益处。
证据基础与方法
该综述基于最近发表的一项研究,其中 AHR 激活是许多病毒逃避 抗病毒药物 免疫的常见策略,但在 RSV 感染期间,AHR 途径下调并发挥保护作用,并且给 RSV 感染小鼠施用膳食 AHR 前配体吲哚-3-甲醇 (I3C) 会产生有益效果。
共识与结论
AHR 激活是许多病毒逃避 抗病毒药物 免疫的常见策略,但在最近发表的一项研究中,我们发现在 RSV 感染期间,AHR 通路下调并发挥保护作用,并且给 RSV 感染小鼠施用膳食 AHR 前配体吲哚-3-甲醇 (I3C) 会产生有益效果。
争议与缺口
尽管最近取得了重大进展,但预防策略仍然存在重大差距,特别是对于年龄较大的婴儿和服务不足的人群,而且迄今为止还没有具体的 治疗 可用。
科研与实践启示
这些天然存在的或营养性配体激活的 AHR 与有益的生理过程相关。值得注意的是,AHR 在肺部等屏障器官中具有关键功能,包括调节 免疫应答 对感染的影响。

Therapeutic potential of nutritional aryl hydrocarbon receptor ligands for respiratory syncytial virus infection: a mini review

Authors: Sydney E. Green, Antonella Casola, Aline Haas de Mello
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infection is still a major cause of hospitalization of young children and older adults. Despite recent significant advances, critical gaps remain in prevention strategies, particularly for older infants and underserved populations, and to date there is no specific treatment available. The aryl hydrocarbon receptor (AHR) is a transcription factor initially identified as mediator of the toxic effects of environmental pollutants that was later found to be also activated by ligands derived from food, the gut microbiome, and host metabolism. AHR activation by these naturally occurring, or nutritional, ligands has been associated with beneficial physiological processes. Notably, AHR has critical functions in barrier organs such as the lungs, including regulation of immune responses to infection. AHR activation is a common strategy for many viruses to evade antiviral immunity, but in a recently published study we found that during RSV infection the AHR pathway is downregulated and plays a protective role, and the administration of the dietary AHR pro-ligand indole-3-carbinol (I3C) to RSV-infected mice led to beneficial effects. Here we review and discuss studies indicating nutritional AHR ligands, particularly compounds derived from cruciferous vegetables or generated during the metabolism of tryptophan, may offer therapeutic benefit for RSV disease, by positively modulating inflammation and reducing excessive immune responses, oxidative stress, and viral replication.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infection is still a major cause of hospitalization of young children and older adults. Here we review and discuss studies indicating nutritional AHR ligands, particularly compounds derived from cruciferous vegetables or generated during the metabolism of tryptophan, may offer therapeutic benefit for RSV disease, by positively modulating inflammation and reducing excessive immune responses, oxidative stress, and viral replication.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a recently published study where AHR activation is a common strategy for many viruses to evade antiviral immunity, but during RSV infection the AHR pathway is downregulated and plays a protective role, and the administration of the dietary AHR pro-ligand indole-3-carbinol (I3C) to RSV-infected mice led to beneficial effects.
Consensus and conclusions
AHR activation is a common strategy for many viruses to evade antiviral immunity, but in a recently published study we found that during RSV infection the AHR pathway is downregulated and plays a protective role, and the administration of the dietary AHR pro-ligand indole-3-carbinol (I3C) to RSV-infected mice led to beneficial effects.
Controversies and gaps
Despite recent significant advances, critical gaps remain in prevention strategies, particularly for older infants and underserved populations, and to date there is no specific treatment available.
Research and practice implications
AHR activation by these naturally occurring, or nutritional, ligands has been associated with beneficial physiological processes. Notably, AHR has critical functions in barrier organs such as the lungs, including regulation of immune responses to infection.

用于呼吸道病原体诊断的 CRISPR/Cas 系统的最新进展

Recent Advances in CRISPR/Cas Systems for Respiratory Pathogen Diagnostics
作者： Yujie Dai, Lingyun Xia, Yufei Yang, Guohong Qiao, Xing Jin, Xuhua Mao
摘要中文翻译早期、快速、准确的检测对于病原体引起的急性呼吸道感染的临床管理和流行病学控制至关重要。微生物培养、血清学检测和 PCR 等传统检测方法在操作和物流方面受到限制,因此不易在现场护理环境中使用。CRISPR/Cas系统是一种适应性原核免疫系统,由成簇规则间隔的短回文重复序列及其相关蛋白组成,已被用作具有可编程序列特异性靶标识别和信号放大附带裂解活性的核酸诊断平台。现有的综述主要集中在Cas酶的分类或扩增策略上;本次综述采取了以病原体为中心的方法,涵盖病毒病原体(SARS-CoV-2、流感病毒、RSV、HAdV和VZV)、细菌病原体(结核分枝杆菌、肺炎链球菌、肺炎支原体和金黄色葡萄球菌)和真菌病原体(烟曲霉和耶氏肺孢子菌)。针对基于 Cas9、Cas12 和 Cas13 的系统评估了等温放大耦合、单容器集成反应设计、无放大数字检测和电化学生物传感器集成等关键技术进步,并概述了其机械基础。目前阻碍临床转化的挑战,例如样本基质干扰、多信号串扰、crRNA 脱靶效应以及缺乏大规模验证研究,均受到严格评估。同时,还提出了便携式、集成且廉价的诊断平台的未来发展方向。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了 CRISPR/Cas 系统在早期、快速和准确检测急性呼吸道感染中的应用,重点关注病毒病原体,包括 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 作为目标实体。核心问题涉及基于 CRISPR/Cas 的诊断在管理 RSV 和相关呼吸道感染的护理点环境中的适用性。
证据基础与方法
这是一项以病原体为中心的综述,评估 CRISPR/Cas 系统的诊断应用。它评估了等温扩增耦合、单容器集成反应设计、无扩增数字检测以及基于 Cas9、Cas12 和 Cas13 系统的电化学生物传感器集成等技术进步。该综述批判性地审视了样品基质干扰、信号串扰、crRNA 脱靶效应以及缺乏大规模验证研究等挑战。
共识与结论
CRISPR/Cas系统提供可编程的序列特异性靶标识别和信号放大的附带裂解活性,使其成为呼吸道病原体核酸诊断的有前景的平台。基于 Cas9、Cas12 和 Cas13 的系统的关键技术进步被强调为开发便携式、集成且廉价的诊断平台的基础。
争议与缺口
该综述指出了阻碍临床转化的关键挑战,包括样本基质干扰、多信号串扰、crRNA 脱靶效应以及缺乏大规模验证研究。这些差距强调了在广泛采用之前需要进一步完善方法和进行实证验证。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先解决样本基质干扰、优化信号特异性并进行大规模验证研究,以实现基于 CRISPR/Cas 的诊断的临床转化。建议开发便携式、集成且廉价的平台,以增强呼吸道病原体检测的护理点适用性。

Recent Advances in CRISPR/Cas Systems for Respiratory Pathogen Diagnostics

Authors: Yujie Dai, Lingyun Xia, Yufei Yang, Guohong Qiao, Xing Jin, Xuhua Mao
Original abstract (English)
Early, rapid, and accurate detection is essential for clinical management and epidemiological control of acute respiratory infections caused by pathogens. Traditional testing methods such as microbial culture, serological testing, and PCR are restrictive in terms of operation and logistics and are therefore not easily used in point-of-care settings. The CRISPR/Cas system is an adaptive prokaryotic immune system composed of clustered regularly interspaced short palindromic repeats and their associated proteins, which has been used as a nucleic acid diagnostic platform with programmable sequence-specific target recognition and signal-amplifying collateral cleavage activity. Existing reviews have mostly focused on the classification of Cas enzymes or amplification strategies; in this review, a pathogen-centric approach was taken, covering viral pathogens (SARS-CoV-2, influenza virus, RSV, HAdV and VZV), bacterial pathogens (Mycobacterium tuberculosis, Streptococcus pneumoniae, Mycoplasma pneumoniae and Staphylococcus aureus) and fungal pathogens (Aspergillus fumigatus and Pneumocystis jirovecii). Key technological advances, such as isothermal amplification coupling, single-vessel integrated reaction designs, amplification-free digital detection, and electrochemical biosensor integration, are evaluated for Cas9-, Cas12-, and Cas13-based systems, with their mechanistic bases outlined. The current challenges that hinder clinical translation, such as sample matrix interference, multiple signal cross-talk, crRNA off-target effects, and the lack of large-scale validation studies, are critically assessed. At the same time, future pathways for portable, integrated, and inexpensive diagnostic platforms are suggested.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the application of CRISPR/Cas systems for early, rapid, and accurate detection of acute respiratory infections, focusing on viral pathogens including respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) as a target entity. The central question addresses the suitability of CRISPR/Cas-based diagnostics for point-of-care settings in managing RSV and related respiratory infections.
Evidence base and method
This is a pathogen-centric review evaluating CRISPR/Cas systems for diagnostic applications. It assesses technological advances such as isothermal amplification coupling, single-vessel integrated reaction designs, amplification-free digital detection, and electrochemical biosensor integration across Cas9-, Cas12-, and Cas13-based systems. The review critically examines challenges including sample matrix interference, signal cross-talk, crRNA off-target effects, and the lack of large-scale validation studies.
Consensus and conclusions
The CRISPR/Cas system offers programmable sequence-specific target recognition and signal-amplifying collateral cleavage activity, making it a promising platform for nucleic acid diagnostics of respiratory pathogens. Key technological advances in Cas9-, Cas12-, and Cas13-based systems are highlighted as foundational for developing portable, integrated, and inexpensive diagnostic platforms.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies critical challenges hindering clinical translation, including sample matrix interference, multiple signal cross-talk, crRNA off-target effects, and the lack of large-scale validation studies. These gaps underscore the need for further methodological refinement and empirical validation before widespread adoption.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize addressing sample matrix interference, optimizing signal specificity, and conducting large-scale validation studies to enable clinical translation of CRISPR/Cas-based diagnostics. Development of portable, integrated, and inexpensive platforms is suggested to enhance point-of-care applicability for respiratory pathogen detection.

呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 英格兰孕产妇 疫苗接种 覆盖范围:2026 年 3 月报告 - gov.uk

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) maternal vaccination coverage in England: March 2026 report - gov.uk
新闻简报英国健康安全局于 2026 年 8 月 6 日发布的这份报告详细介绍了截至 2026 年 3 月英国 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 孕产妇 疫苗接种 覆盖范围。报告强调,在调查月份内分娩的妇女中有 62.2% 接受了 RSV 疫苗,并指出了地区和种族差异。西南地区(72.5%)和亚洲或亚洲英籍华人族群(76.3%)的使用率最高,最低的是伦敦(51.3%)和黑人加勒比族群(33.6%)。该报告强调,疫苗 的吸收数据现在基于超过 97.5% 的全科医生实践的信息,提高了分析的准确性。该计划旨在减轻 RSV 相关疾病、住院和死亡的负担,特别是六个月以下的婴儿和老年人。研究结果是官方统计数据发布的一部分,旨在为公共卫生策略提供信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 8 月 6 日。该报告涵盖截至 2026 年 3 月的数据,测量了在该计划第 19 个月分娩的女性的 疫苗 吸收率。
地点与对象
英格兰,重点关注 NHS 委托地区、综合护理委员会 (ICB) 和地方当局。该报告还包括按族裔群体划分的数据。
事件
该报告介绍了英格兰每月 RSV 孕产妇 疫苗接种 覆盖率,强调了 2026 年 3 月分娩的孕妇中 疫苗 的使用情况。这是英国健康安全局发布的官方统计数据。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年 3 月,孕妇 RSV 疫苗 的吸收率为 62.2%,地区差异高达 21.2 个百分点,种族差异高达 42.7 个百分点。该计划旨在减轻 RSV 相关疾病、住院和死亡的负担,特别是六个月以下的婴儿和老年人。
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告是官方发布的统计数据,包含来自两大全科医生IT供应商的数据,覆盖了英格兰98.9%的全科医生。由于数据质量的提高,它提供了更具代表性的 疫苗 吸收分析。研究结果基于国家免疫规划的数据。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) maternal vaccination coverage in England: March 2026 report - gov.uk

News Brief
This report from the UK Health Security Agency, published on 6 August 2026, details the respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) maternal vaccination coverage in England up to March 2026. It highlights that 62.2% of women who gave birth during the survey month received the RSV vaccine, with regional and ethnic variations noted. The highest uptake was observed in the South West region (72.5%) and the Asian or Asian British - Chinese ethnic group (76.3%), while the lowest was in London (51.3%) and the Black - Caribbean ethnic group (33.6%). The report emphasizes that vaccine uptake data is now based on information from over 97.5% of GP practices, improving the accuracy of the analysis. The programme aims to reduce the burden of RSV-related illness, hospitalisations, and deaths, especially among infants under six months and older adults. The findings are part of an official statistics release and are intended to inform public health strategies.

This report includes vaccine uptake estimates for pregnant women who delivered in the nineteenth month of the maternal vaccination programme in March 2026. Main points The main messages of this report are: 62.2% (25,566 out of 41,128) of women that reported giving birth during the survey month received a RSV vaccine uptake varied by NHS commissioning region, with the highest uptake reported in the South West region (72.5%) and the lowest in the London region (51.3%) uptake varied across integrated care boards (ICBs) with the highest uptake reported in the Cornwall and the Isles of Scilly ICB (77.8%) and the lowest in Birmingham and Solihull (39.3%) uptake varied across local authorities, with the highest uptake reported in the West Berkshire (84.0%) and the lowest in Enfield (34.3%) uptake varied by ethnic group, with the highest uptake reported among the Asian or Asian British - Chinese ethnic group (76.3%) and the lowest among the Black - Caribbean ethnic group (33.6%) Help us improve this resource This vaccine uptake report has been designated as an Official Statistics release. Analysis of maternal RSV vaccine uptake Maternal uptake of the RSV vaccine is assessed monthly to monitor the reach and impact of the national immunisation programme. Given the programme’s design, uptake is defined as the proportion of pregnant women who delivered in the reporting month and had received the RSV vaccine during pregnancy. This metric serves as a proxy for vaccine coverage by infant birth month, reflecting the extent of passive immunity conferred to newborns. This monthly report presents RSV vaccine uptake for women who delivered in the nineteenth month of the RSV vaccination programme in March 2026. The monthly uptake trend among pregnant women who have delivered since the programme’s launch is presented in Figure 1. Overall, the monthly RSV maternal vaccine uptake for March 2026 was 62.2%. RSV uptake increased in the first few months of the programme and has remained steady since October 2025. Figure 1. RSV vaccine uptake (%) trend among pregnant women by month of delivery, measured in March 2026 Note 1: the vertical lines represent a change in the percentage of GP (general practitioner) practices used to calculate RSV vaccine uptake. Up to December 2024, vaccine uptake was estimated based on data from a single supplier covering fewer than 40.5% of GP practices. From January 2025 onwards, vaccine uptake was based on data from more than 97.5% of GP practices and is more representative of national vaccination uptake. This month’s report includes data from both major GP IT suppliers, significantly expanding the data available in earliest reports (September to December 2024). It includes data from 98.9% of GPs in England, up from 40.4% of participating GPs previously in the first 4 months (September to December 2024) of the vaccination programme. This has been made possible by improvements in data quality and permits a more representative analysis of uptake and trends across England. To further explore variation in uptake, Table 1 presents uptake by commissioning region, while Table 2 provides the breakdown by ethnicity. As shown in Table 1, there are notable regional differences in uptake, with variation of up to 21.2 percentage points between NHS commissioning regions. Table 1. The ‘Any other ethnicity code’ category includes individuals whose ethnicity has been recorded using non-standard or legacy codes (that is, codes not aligned with the 2001 or 2011 Census classifications). The map in figure 2 shows RSV uptake by ICB. Uptake varied across ICBs by 38.5 percentage points with the highest uptake reported in Cornwall and the Isles of Scilly (77.8) and the lowest uptake reported in Birmingham and Solihull (39.3). RSV vaccine uptake (%) among pregnant women delivering in March 2026, by ICBs Across the local authorities, uptake differed by 49.7 percentage points with the highest uptake reported in West Berkshire (84.0%) and the lowest uptake reported in Enfield (34.3%). The variation across the local authority are presented in the map shown in figure 3. Figure 3. RSV vaccine uptake (%) among pregnant women delivering in March 2026, by local authority Further detail, including uptake figures broken down by local authority and ICB, can be found in the March 2026 supplementary data tables. Since the RSV maternal vaccination programme has now been running for nineteen months (up to the reporting month), an increasing number of pregnant women are receiving the vaccine during their eligible window. This growing awareness and notification from gestation week 20 onwards have contributed to a gradual increase in monthly vaccine uptake from 40.5% in September 2024 to the current figure reported for March 2026. As the programme continues and more women are informed earlier in their pregnancies, monthly vaccination uptake is expected to gradually increase. Data sources and methodology More information is available in our quality and methodology information (QMI) report. The maternal RSV vaccination report will be released monthly, presenting vaccination uptake data only for the specific reporting month, without cumulative figures from previous months. The reporting period for the current report is from 1 March 2026 to 31 March 2026. This report focuses on pregnant women who delivered in the survey month, regardless of gestational age at birth. GP-level RSV vaccine uptake data is automatically uploaded via participating GP IT suppliers to the ImmForm website each month. ImmForm data is validated and analysed by the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) to check data completeness, query any anomalous data and describe epidemiological trends. Since September 2024, the reporting data collected includes: denominator: number of women who delivered in the survey month, excluding miscarriages and stillbirths, regardless of gestational age numerator: number of women receiving RSV vaccination from week 28 of pregnancy up until delivery refusals: number of women in the denominator who refused RSV vaccination between week 28 of pregnancy and delivery To ensure accurate denominators are extracted from GP IT systems by the automated survey and precise uptake estimates are calculated, the methodology uses a monthly data extraction process with a three-month reporting lag to allow enough time for relevant information to be recorded. For example, data from September 2024 was extracted on 1 December, 2024. The data presented in this report for March 2026 is based on data received from both major GP IT suppliers, accounting for 98.9% of all national GP practices participating during the reporting month. The April 2026 maternal RSV vaccination uptake report is scheduled to be released on 3 September 2026. Background information The RSV vaccine has been offered to pregnant women in England since September 2024 to address the significant burden of RSV-related illness, hospitalisations and deaths, particularly among infants under six months of age and older adults, who are at increased risk of lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) (see the Green Book on immunisation). In 2023, the Joint Committee on Vaccination and Immunisation (JCVI) issued a full statement recommending the implementation of a maternal RSV vaccination programme. This recommendation was based on robust evidence demonstrating the safety and effectiveness of RSV vaccines in protecting infants during their most vulnerable period. The vaccine is recommended as a year-round programme to be administered from 28 weeks of gestation onwards. This timing aligns with the optimal window for passive antibody transfer to the foetus, ensuring effective protection against severe RSV-related illness. From 1 September 2024 (the programme start date), all women who are at least 28 weeks pregnant are advised to receive the vaccine as soon as possible, with vaccination continuing to be recommended for all eligible women throughout pregnancy up until delivery. Additionally, the vaccine will be reoffered for every subsequent pregnancy to maintain consistent coverage and protection. This monthly report includes vaccine uptake data for women who delivered in March 2026. Programme delivery The programme is delivered through GP and maternity services, ensuring accessible vaccination opportunities for eligible pregnant women across England. In addition, the RSV vaccine is available through the community pharmacy delivery model at approximately 37 sites across two ICBs in the East of England. It is also available at up to 200 additional community pharmacy sites across the Midlands, North West, and London commissioning regions during the 2025 to 2026 expansion phase. Previous monthly reports are available at RSV maternal vaccination coverage in England. Published 6 August 2026. The report covers data up to March 2026, with vaccine uptake measured for women who delivered in the nineteenth month of the programme.

England, focusing on NHS commissioning regions, integrated care boards (ICBs), and local authorities. The report also includes data by ethnic group.

呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 英格兰老年人 疫苗接种 覆盖范围:2026 年 6 月报告 - gov.uk

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) older adults vaccination coverage in England: June 2026 report - gov.uk
新闻简报英国健康安全局于 2026 年 8 月 6 日报告称,根据 2024 年 9 月 1 日启动的国家免疫计划,截至 2026 年 6 月 30 日,英格兰 63.5% 的符合条件的老年人已接种 RSV 疫苗。补充 队列研究 的覆盖率 (69.2%) 高于常规 队列研究 (48.3%),反映出该计划分阶段向开始日期前年龄为 75-79 岁的人士以及之后年满 75 岁的人士推出。地区采用率从西南部的 68.3% 到伦敦的 49.4%,在性别、贫困、种族和综合护理委员会区域方面存在显着差异。该报告被指定为官方统计数据,跟踪每月的累积使用情况,并强调老年人在获得 疫苗接种 方面持续存在的不平等。

2026 年 8 月 6 日发布;数据期间2024年9月1日至2026年6月30日。

英格兰;符合资格的老年人(常规 队列研究:2024 年 9 月 1 日或之后年满 75 岁的个人;追赶 队列研究:2024 年 9 月 1 日之前年龄为 75-79 岁的个人)。

英国健康安全局发布的关于英格兰老年人 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 疫苗接种 吸收率的官方统计报告。

截至 2026 年 6 月 30 日,符合条件的老年人 疫苗 的总体使用率达到 63.5%;追赶型 队列研究 吸收率 69.2%,常规 队列研究 48.3%。地区差异:西南地区最高(68.3%),伦敦最低(49.4%)。按性别划分:男性 65.4%,女性 61.7%。按剥夺程度划分:最贫困的十分之一为 50.3%,最贫困的为 71.6%。按种族划分:英国白人最高(67.0%),亚洲或亚裔英国-巴基斯坦人最低(25.1%)。按 ICB 划分:NHS 巴斯、东北萨默塞特、斯温顿和威尔特郡最高 (70.5%),NHS 东北伦敦最低 (46%)。

A high priorityPublic-health news

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) older adults vaccination coverage in England: June 2026 report - gov.uk

News Brief
The UK Health Security Agency reported on 6 August 2026 that 63.5% of eligible older adults in England had received an RSV vaccine by 30 June 2026 under the national immunisation programme launched on 1 September 2024. Coverage was higher in the catch-up cohort (69.2%) than in the routine cohort (48.3%), reflecting the programme’s staged rollout to those aged 75–79 before the start date and those turning 75 thereafter. Regional uptake ranged from 68.3% in the South West to 49.4% in London, with notable disparities by sex, deprivation, ethnicity, and integrated care board area. The report, designated as official statistics, tracks cumulative uptake monthly and highlights persistent inequities in access to vaccination among older populations.

This report includes vaccine uptake estimates for the routine cohort (individuals who turned 75 years of age since the programme start date of 1 September 2024) and the catch-up cohort (defined as individuals aged 75 to 79 years before 1 September 2024). Uptake data for both cohorts is reported as the cumulative figure measured up to 30 June 2026. Main points Between 1 September 2024 and 30 June 2026: in the eligible older adults (routine and catch-up cohort), the overall vaccine uptake reached 63.5%, decreasing by 0.1 percentage points from 63.6% reported in May 2026 vaccine uptake in the catch-up cohort reached 69.2%, increasing by 0.2 percentage points from the 69.0% reported in May 2026 vaccine uptake in the routine cohort reached 48.3%, decreasing 0.5 percentage points in this cohort (from 48.8% reported in May 2026) in all the eligible older adults’ cohorts, regional vaccine uptake was highest in the South West (68.3%) and lowest in London (49.4%) uptake in males was 65.4% and in females was 61.7% uptake in the most deprived decile was 50.3% and in the least deprived decile was 71.6% uptake varied by ethnic group, with the highest uptake reported among White - British (67.0%) and the lowest among Asian or Asian British - Pakistani (25.1%) uptake varied by integrated care board (ICB), with the highest vaccine uptake in the NHS Bath and North East Somerset, Swindon and Wiltshire ICB (70.5%) and lowest in the NHS North East London ICB (46%) Help us improve this resource This vaccine uptake report has been designated as an official statistics release. Analysis of RSV vaccine uptake by cohort RSV vaccine uptake in older adults is described in terms of cohorts. This is where a cohort is defined in reference to the age at the programme start date (September 1, 2024, as described in the Green Book on immunisation against infectious disease). The eligible older adults are defined into 2 cohorts comprising the catch-up cohorts (a birth group cohort of those who were aged 75 to 79 years before the programme start date) and the routine cohort (defined dynamically as those that first became eligible by turning 75 years on or after the programme start date). Owing to the dynamic nature of the routine cohort, the eligible individuals in this cohort will increase progressively through the year as more individuals become eligible. The current cumulative vaccine uptake, dates of birth and eligibility definitions for these cohorts are described in Table 1. Cumulative uptake in the catch-up cohorts of the RSV vaccination programme for older adults has been steadily increasing since the programme’s start. Uptake has risen from 22.6% in September 2024 to 63.5% by the end of June 2026. As the older adults remain eligible until they turn 80 years of age, it is expected that uptake in this cohort will increase progressively as they remain eligible for longer and have more opportunities to be vaccinated. Table 1. Cumulative RSV vaccine uptake for older adults in the routine and catch-up cohorts, measured at 30 June 2026 Cohort group Birth range of cohort Age turned in September 2024 Uptake (%) Routine cohort Adults turning 75 years old on or after 1 September 2024 75 48.3 Catch-up cohort 1 1 September 1948 to 31 August 1949 75 68.5 Catch-up cohort 2 1 September 1947 to 31 August 1948 76 69.1 Catch-up cohort 3 1 September 1946 to 31 August 1947 77 70.0 Catch-up cohort 4 1 September 1945 to 31 August 1946 78 69.8 Catch-up cohort 5 1 September 1944 to 31 August 1945 79 68.7 Total 63.5 The cumulative vaccine uptake among all eligible older adults (routine and catch-up cohorts) was 63.5% in the reporting month. This represented a 0.1 percentage point decrease compared with May 2026 (63.6%). The uptake within the stable, fixed birth-year catch-up cohort has shown a steady increase (increasing by 0.2 percentage points from the 69.2% reported in May 2026). This provides a clearer picture of progress unaffected by population changes and rolling eligibility. The combined uptake trend among all eligible older adults (catch-up and routine cohorts) is shown in Figure 1, while the trend in RSV cumulative uptake by cohort for the catch-up and routine cohorts is shown in Figure 2. Figure 1. Cumulative RSV vaccine uptake for older adults (both routine and catch-up cohorts combined), measured at 30 June 2026 As shown in Figure 2, uptake differed between the cohorts, reflecting variations in eligibility patterns. The routine cohort achieved a cumulative uptake of 48.3% which was lower than the uptake in the catch-up cohort (69.2%). These differences highlight the dynamic nature of the routine cohort, in which individuals become eligible over time, compared with the more static population of the catch-up cohort during the reporting period. Among the routine cohort, the vaccine uptake decreased by 0.5 percentage points from 48.8% in May 2026 to 48.3% in June 2026. In addition, the higher uptake among individuals who turned 75 years of age in September 2024 compared to those turning 75 years of age in the current reporting month reflects the natural lag between becoming eligible, receiving an invitation, and presenting for vaccination. This time-dependent pattern is expected and consistent with the rolling eligibility of people in this cohort and highlights the importance of allowing sufficient time for individuals to engage with the vaccination programme. These dynamics underscore why fluctuations in overall uptake should be interpreted with caution and why separate reporting for routine and catch-up cohorts provides a more accurate assessment of the programme performance. Figure 2. Cumulative RSV vaccine uptake comparing routine and catch-up cohorts, measured at 30 June 2026 To illustrate the rolling eligibility within the routine cohort, Figure 3 presents uptake by month of birth, showing uptake patterns as individuals reach the age of 75 years and become eligible. The oldest people in the routine cohort (those who reached the age of 75 years in September 2024) have had the most time to receive a notification and attend their vaccination appointments. As a result, their uptake is higher compared to those who only just reached the age of 75 years in the current reporting month. Figure 3. RSV vaccine uptake for adults in the routine cohorts (adult turning 75 years after 1 September 2024) by month of birth, measured at 30 June 2026 As shown in Table 2, uptake varied across the NHS commissioning regions, with the highest uptake reported in the South West (68.3%) while the lowest was reported in London (49.4%). Map of RSV vaccine uptake in older adults (including catch-up and routine cohorts) by ICB, measured at 30 June 2026 Further detail, including uptake figures broken down by local authority and ICB, can be found in the June 2026 supplementary data tables. Data sources and methodology More information is available in our quality and methodology information (QMI) report. This is the twenty-first in the series of older-adult RSV vaccination uptake reports that will be released monthly, providing cumulative vaccination uptake data since the start of the campaign. The reporting period in the current report is from 1 September 2024 to June 2026. Data is collected through the Immunisation Information System (IIS), which holds denominator data and captures vaccination events from GP (general practitioner) practices and participating community pharmacies across England, based on data provided by NHS England. Vaccine uptake is calculated by dividing the number of eligible older adults who have received the RSV vaccine by the total number of individuals eligible for the vaccination. The vaccine uptake is presented by cohort (routine versus catch-up) and for all older adults combined. Where relevant, figures also describe uptake within the routine cohort by month of birth to illustrate uptake as individuals become newly eligible when aged 75 years. The data was extracted on 28 July 2026 to include vaccinations administered up to midnight of the last day of the reporting month (30 June 2026). The 5 catch-up cohorts are defined by their birth year and age as of the programme start date. A more detailed breakdown of the different cohorts can be found in Table 1. This report presents the vaccine uptake in both the routine and catch-up cohorts. The routine cohort are defined as those turning 75 years of age since the programme’s start date. This cohort is currently comprised of those that are just turning 75 years of age, and those that are currently aged 76 years. As individuals in this cohorts are gradually becoming eligible as the vaccination programme progresses, some have not yet had sufficient time to be notified and present themselves for vaccination due to the rolling eligibility in this cohort. Data extraction note: population estimates for denominator data and vaccination records were extracted from the IIS on 28 July 2026 for vaccine events up to 30 June 2026 the data for the new RSV programme relies on new data flows and is, therefore, provisional while enhanced data validation checks are being conducted; any changes to historic figures will be reflected in the most recent publication The July older adult RSV vaccination uptake report is scheduled to be released on 3 September 2026. Birth cohorts Table 5. Birth range and RSV vaccine eligibility dates for routine and catch-up cohorts of older adult populations, measured at 30 June 2026 Cohort group Birth range of cohort Age turned since September 2024 Routine cohort Adults turning 75 years old on or after 1 September 2024 75 Catch-up cohort 1 1 September 1948 to 31 August 1949 75 Catch-up cohort 2 1 September 1947 to 31 August 1948 76 Catch-up cohort 3 1 September 1946 to 31 August 1947 77 Catch-up cohort 4 1 September 1945 to 31 August 1946 78 Catch-up cohort 5 1 September 1944 to 31 August 1945 79 Background information The RSV vaccination programme was introduced in September 2024, targeting older adults as part of a national public health initiative. This routine programme is offered to older adults (divided into the routine and catch-up cohort) upon turning the age of 75 years, and who remain eligible until they turn 80 years of age. The RSV vaccination programme for older adults in England began on 1 September 2024 as a single-dose offer. Eligibility is defined at the programme start date and comprises 2 groups (a routine cohort and catch-up cohort). The populations eligible for the programme are divided into 2 groups defined in the Green Book on immunisation against infectious disease based on their age on or after the programme start date, as follows: rolling, year-round eligibility in adults turning 75 years on or after the programme start date (1 September 2024): the routine cohort adults already aged 75 to 79 years before the programme start date (comprising 5 catch-up cohorts) are eligible up until their 80th birthday Those who reach the age of 80 years in the first year of the programme and have not been vaccinated still remain eligible until the present date of the programme. The primary aim of the older adult RSV programme is to reduce severe RSV disease, hospitalisations and complications among older adults, particularly those aged 75 years and over. Programme uptake is reported as cumulative uptake by week, using ISO week definitions, from...
View five news elements
Time
Published 6 August 2026; data period 1 September 2024 to 30 June 2026.
Location and population
England; eligible older adults (routine cohort: individuals turning 75 on or after 1 September 2024; catch-up cohort: individuals aged 75–79 before 1 September 2024).
Event
Official statistics report on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccination uptake among older adults in England, published by the UK Health Security Agency.
Scale, impact and risk
Overall vaccine uptake among eligible older adults reached 63.5% by 30 June 2026; catch-up cohort uptake 69.2%, routine cohort 48.3%. Regional variation: highest in South West (68.3%), lowest in London (49.4%). Uptake by sex: males 65.4%, females 61.7%. By deprivation: most deprived decile 50.3%, least deprived 71.6%. By ethnicity: highest in White-British (67.0%), lowest in Asian or Asian British-Pakistani (25.1%). By ICB: highest in NHS Bath and North East Somerset, Swindon and Wiltshire (70.5%), lowest in NHS North East London (46%).
Response, status and uncertainty
Report designated as official statistics; cumulative uptake data presented monthly; programme ongoing with dynamic eligibility for routine cohort; regional, demographic, and ethnic disparities noted; no explicit risk assessment provided in the evidence.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

在突破性感染中检测到 RSV-B 对 尼塞韦单抗 的耐药性 - EMJ

RSV-B Resistance to Nirsevimab Detected in Breakthrough Infections - EMJ
新闻简报法国的一项全国性观察性研究在 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节爆发性感染的婴儿中检测到了 尼塞韦单抗 耐药性 呼吸道合胞病毒 B (RSV-B) 变异。在 1,023 名确诊患有 RSV 的婴儿中,419 名接受过 尼塞韦单抗 预防,439 名未接受。12.5% 的 RSV-B 突破病例中发现了耐药性,主要涉及病毒 F 蛋白残基 208 的替换,而只有 1.0% 的 RSV-A 突破感染显示出耐药性。至关重要的是,在未经治疗的婴儿中没有发现耐药菌株,这表明在预防的选择性压力下出现了耐药性。作者强调,耐药性仍然仅限于少数突破性感染,并不意味着 治疗 广泛失败,但他们呼吁扩大 基因组监测 来追踪病毒 进化 并为未来的单克隆抗体设计提供信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-10;赛事期间 2024–2025 RSV 赛季。
地点与对象
法国;实验室确诊的 RSV 感染的一岁或以下婴儿。
事件
在突破性感染中检测到 尼塞韦单抗 耐药 RSV-B 变种的首个大规模真实世界证据;研究作者强调,阻力仍然仅限于突破性案例。
规模、影响与风险
对 1,023 例婴儿 RSV 病例进行了测序;尼塞韦单抗 接受者中出现 419 例突破性感染,非接受者中出现 439 例突破性感染;12.5% 的 RSV-B 突破性感染携带耐药性(RSV-A 为 1.0%);在未经治疗的婴儿中未检测到耐药菌株;尽管出现了耐药性,尼塞韦单抗 的总体临床疗效仍然很高。
应对、状态与不确定性
多中心 POLYRES-2 观察研究;基因组监测与中和测试相结合;作者建议将基因组测序整合到常规 监测 中,以监测新出现的逃逸 突变 并指导未来的单克隆抗体开发;抗性变种可能会持续到 RSV 季节过后。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

RSV-B Resistance to Nirsevimab Detected in Breakthrough Infections - EMJ

News Brief
A nationwide observational study in France has detected nirsevimab-resistant respiratory syncytial virus B (RSV-B) variants in infants with breakthrough infections during the 2024–2025 RSV season. Among 1,023 infants with confirmed RSV, 419 had received nirsevimab prophylaxis and 439 had not. Resistance was identified in 12.5% of RSV-B breakthrough cases, primarily involving substitutions at residue 208 of the viral F protein, while only 1.0% of RSV-A breakthrough infections showed resistance. Crucially, no resistant strains were found in untreated infants, indicating resistance emerged under selective pressure from prophylaxis. The authors stress that resistance remains confined to a minority of breakthrough infections and does not signal widespread treatment failure, but they call for expanded genomic surveillance to track viral evolution and inform future monoclonal antibody design.
Original source text (English)
Key Summary: - RSV-B resistance emerged in breakthrough infections. - No resistant viruses found in untreated infants. - Genomic surveillance remains essential. Nirsevimab-resistant respiratory syncytial virus B (RSV-B) variants have been identified in infants with breakthrough infections during routine clinical use, according to a nationwide observational study from France. The findings provide the first large-scale real-world evidence that resistance to the long-acting monoclonal antibody can emerge following prophylaxis, although researchers emphasise that resistant viruses remain confined to breakthrough cases. Nirsevimab, which targets a conserved region of the RSV prefusion F protein, has transformed prevention of severe RSV disease in infants since its introduction. Clinical trials and early implementation studies demonstrated high efficacy against RSV-associated hospitalisation, but concerns have persisted over whether widespread use could drive viral evolution. The multicentre POLYRES-2 study examined RSV infections in 1,023 infants aged one year or younger who tested positive for RSV during the 2024–2025 season in France, when RSV-B predominated nationally. Researchers successfully generated full-length viral genome sequences for 858 cases, including 419 breakthrough infections in infants who had received nirsevimab and 439 infections in infants who had not received the antibody. Viral sequencing was combined with laboratory neutralisation testing to determine whether identified mutations reduced susceptibility to nirsevimab. Resistance-associated substitutions were uncommon in RSV-A, occurring in just 1.0% of breakthrough infections. In contrast, resistant variants were identified in 12.5% of RSV-B breakthrough infections. More than half of resistant RSV-B viruses carried mutations affecting amino acid residue 208 of the viral F protein, while researchers also identified several previously unreported mutations capable of reducing susceptibility to nirsevimab. Importantly, no resistant RSV strains were detected in infants who had not received nirsevimab, suggesting these mutations emerged under selective pressure following prophylaxis rather than circulating widely in the community. One notable finding was the detection of an RSV-B variant carrying the F:N208S substitution almost one year after administration of nirsevimab. The authors suggest this demonstrates that resistant viruses can persist longer than previously anticipated, highlighting the importance of monitoring viral evolution beyond the immediate RSV season. Despite the emergence of resistance, the study does not suggest widespread treatment failure. Instead, resistance remained confined to a relatively small proportion of breakthrough infections, and the overall clinical effectiveness of nirsevimab remains high. The researchers conclude that integrating genomic sequencing with routine clinical surveillance will be essential as RSV immunisation programmes continue to expand. Monitoring resistant variants in real-world settings could help identify emerging escape mutations early, inform future monoclonal antibody development, and ensure preventive strategies remain effective against evolving RSV strains. While the findings warrant continued vigilance, the authors emphasise that the overall benefits of nirsevimab prophylaxis remain substantial, with resistance currently representing only a minority of breakthrough RSV-B infections. Reference Fourati S et al. Real-world emergence of nirsevimab resistance in breakthrough infections with respiratory syncytial virus-B: a multicentre observational study in France. The Lancet Microbe. 2026;DOI: 10.1016/j.lanmic.2026.101353. - Author: Each article is made available under the terms of the Creative Commons Attribution-Non Commercial 4.0 License. Nirsevimab-resistant respiratory syncytial virus B (RSV-B) variants have been identified in infants with breakthrough infections during routine clinical use, according to a nationwide observational study from France. The findings provide the first large-scale real-world evidence that resistance to the long-acting monoclonal antibody can emerge following prophylaxis, although researchers emphasise that resistant viruses remain confined to breakthrough cases. Nirsevimab, which targets a conserved region of the RSV prefusion F protein, has transformed prevention of severe RSV disease in infants since its introduction. Clinical trials and early implementation studies demonstrated high efficacy against RSV-associated hospitalisation, but concerns have persisted over whether widespread use could drive viral evolution. Researchers analysed more than 1,000 infant RSV cases. The multicentre POLYRES-2 study examined RSV infections in 1,023 infants aged one year or younger who tested positive for RSV during the 2024–2025 season in France, when RSV-B predominated nationally. Researchers successfully generated full-length viral genome sequences for 858 cases, including 419 breakthrough infections in infants who had received nirsevimab and 439 infections in infants who had not received the antibody. Viral sequencing was combined with laboratory neutralisation testing to determine whether identified mutations reduced susceptibility to nirsevimab. Resistance was primarily detected in RSV-B. Resistance-associated substitutions were uncommon in RSV-A, occurring in just 1.0% of breakthrough infections. In contrast, resistant variants were identified in 12.5% of RSV-B breakthrough infections. More than half of resistant RSV-B viruses carried mutations affecting amino acid residue 208 of the viral F protein, while researchers also identified several previously unreported mutations capable of reducing susceptibility to nirsevimab. Importantly, no resistant RSV strains were detected in infants who had not received nirsevimab, suggesting these mutations emerged under selective pressure following prophylaxis rather than circulating widely in the community. Some resistant variants persisted long after prophylaxis. One notable finding was the detection of an RSV-B variant carrying the F:N208S substitution almost one year after administration of nirsevimab. The authors suggest this demonstrates that resistant viruses can persist longer than previously anticipated, highlighting the importance of monitoring viral evolution beyond the immediate RSV season. Despite the emergence of resistance, the study does not suggest widespread treatment failure. Instead, resistance remained confined to a relatively small proportion of breakthrough infections, and the overall clinical effectiveness of nirsevimab remains high. Genomic surveillance will be critical. The researchers conclude that integrating genomic sequencing with routine clinical surveillance will be essential as RSV immunisation programmes continue to expand. Monitoring resistant variants in real-world settings could help identify emerging escape mutations early, inform future monoclonal antibody development, and ensure preventive strategies remain effective against evolving RSV strains. While the findings warrant continued vigilance, the authors emphasise that the overall benefits of nirsevimab prophylaxis remain substantial, with resistance currently representing only a minority of breakthrough RSV-B infections.
