家长敦促在流感、Covid-19 和 RSV 感染病例中为儿童接种疫苗 - 《南华早报》

Parents urged to vaccinate children amid flu, Covid-19 and RSV infection cases - South China Morning Post
新闻简报Hong Kong is facing a triple threat from respiratory infections, including influenza, Covid-19, and RSV, prompting parents to be urged to vaccinate their children. Dr Mike Kwan Yat-wah, an honorary clinical associate professor at the University of Hong Kong, warned that the current summer flu season has not yet reached its peak, with the first child death from flu reported in early August. Influenza activity remains high, with 9.94% of respiratory specimens testing positive in the week ending August 1. RSV infections have also increased since late June, peaking at 323 cases in late July. The government has procured 1.3 million doses of flu vaccines for the 2026-27 season, with Sinovac and Sanofi supplying most of the doses. However, the city is under investigation for the triple threat, and the timing of the flu peak remains uncertain. The government has also announced a temporary suspension of Covid-19 vaccinations for people aged 12 or above after September 5, as current vaccine supplies expire.
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-08-08。事件日期没有明确说明,但流感季节从 7 月 2 日开始,文章发表四天后,即 8 月 1 日,报告了第一例因流感死亡的儿童。
地点与对象
香港,重点关注儿童和弱势群体,如老年人、慢性病患者以及六个月前感染该病毒的人。
事件
由于香港面临流感、Covid-19 和 RSV 的三重威胁,我们敦促家长为儿童接种呼吸道感染疫苗,包括 RSV。这是香港大学儿科及青少年医学系名誉临床副教授关日华医生的推荐。
规模、影响与风险
流感活动仍然很高,截至 8 月 1 日的一周,9.94% 的呼吸道样本检测出流感阳性。RSV 感染自 6 月下旬以来有所上升,在 7 月最后第二周达到峰值,达到 323 例。据报道,今年首例致命的儿科流感病例,涉及一名感染甲型流感(H3)的七岁男孩。
应对、状态与不确定性
The government has procured 1.3 million doses of flu vaccines for the 2026-27 season, with Sinovac and Sanofi supplying most of the doses. However, the city is under investigation for the triple threat of respiratory infections, and the timing of the flu peak remains unclear. The Covid-19 vaccination programme for people aged 12 or above will be temporarily suspended after September 5.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Parents urged to vaccinate children amid flu, Covid-19 and RSV infection cases - South China Morning Post

News Brief
Hong Kong is facing a triple threat from respiratory infections, including influenza, Covid-19, and RSV, prompting parents to be urged to vaccinate their children. Dr Mike Kwan Yat-wah, an honorary clinical associate professor at the University of Hong Kong, warned that the current summer flu season has not yet reached its peak, with the first child death from flu reported in early August. Influenza activity remains high, with 9.94% of respiratory specimens testing positive in the week ending August 1. RSV infections have also increased since late June, peaking at 323 cases in late July. The government has procured 1.3 million doses of flu vaccines for the 2026-27 season, with Sinovac and Sanofi supplying most of the doses. However, the city is under investigation for the triple threat, and the timing of the flu peak remains uncertain. The government has also announced a temporary suspension of Covid-19 vaccinations for people aged 12 or above after September 5, as current vaccine supplies expire.
Original source text (English)
Hong Kong parents should have their children vaccinated against respiratory infections as the city faces a triple threat from influenza, Covid-19 and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), a medical expert has said. Dr Mike Kwan Yat-wah, an honorary clinical associate professor in the University of Hong Kong’s department of paediatrics and adolescent medicine, warned on Saturday that the current summer flu season had yet to reach its peak. His remarks came four days after the city recorded its first child death from flu this year. “The number [of flu cases] remains high and there is no sign of it dropping yet, therefore it is unclear when the peak will arrive,” he said. Covid-19 and RSV cases were also at high levels, Kwan said, warning that Hong Kong was under threat from three types of infections. “We are now under the triple attack of flu, Covid-19 and RSV ... this triple whammy is happening in Hong Kong and neighbouring areas,” he said. He urged parents to have their children vaccinated if they had not already done so. View in App READ FULL ARTICLE Kwan said children could still be protected by receiving flu jabs for the 2025-26 season, before receiving vaccines for the 2026-27 season under the government’s vaccination scheme. “Parents need not hesitate ... Vaccines for [these two seasons] are different. There is also no need to space them apart for a specific period of time,” he said. Covid-19 vaccine supplies for young children have been temporarily suspended because the jabs have expired. But Kwan said other vulnerable groups, including elderly people, chronically ill patients and those who had caught the virus more than six months earlier, should still get inoculated. According to the latest data from the Centre for Health Protection, influenza activity in Hong Kong remained high after the summer flu season began on July 2. Of respiratory specimens handled by the public laboratory in the week ending August 1, 9.94 per cent tested positive for flu, slightly lower than the 10.65 per cent recorded the previous week. Twenty flu outbreaks were recorded in various institutions last week, mostly in care homes for the elderly. Covid-19 activity also remained elevated, according to the centre’s data. Infections began rising in June, with the public laboratory detecting 700 new cases in the week from July 26 to August 1. Hong Kong recorded 20 flu and 18 Covid-19 outbreaks last week as respiratory infections remained at high levels. Photo: Edmond So Eighteen Covid-19 outbreaks were reported in homes for elderly and disabled people over the same period. The laboratory has also recorded a rise in RSV infections since late June, when the number of detected cases exceeded 200, compared with a few dozen during the non-peak period. The number peaked at 323 in the second last week of July before falling slightly to 265 last week. On Tuesday, the city reported its first fatal paediatric flu case of the year, involving a seven-year-old boy who had contracted influenza A (H3). The boy, who had been in good health and vaccinated, first developed symptoms on July 27. “I hope this serious case does not undermine public confidence in vaccines. It is an isolated case,” Kwan said. Without commenting on the specific case, Kwan said some people’s immunity might weaken several months after vaccination. The city’s 2025-26 vaccination scheme began in September last year. He added that some people might be born with weaker immunity because of genetic predispositions. Kwan said experts might also need to analyse whether the currently circulating H3 strain had undergone genetic changes that could cause more severe reactions in patients. The Department of Health said on Wednesday that the government had procured 1.3 million doses of flu vaccines for the 2026-27 season. About 530,000 doses of inactivated vaccine from Sinovac will arrive in Hong Kong from later this month. Another batch of around 660,000 doses of inactivated vaccine from Sanofi will be delivered from mid-October. The remaining supplies include about 60,000 doses of nasal spray vaccine from AstraZeneca and another 60,000 doses of recombinant vaccine from Sanofi. The government has also announced that Covid-19 jabs offered to people aged 12 or above under its vaccination programme will be temporarily suspended after September 5, when the current batch expires. Supplies for children aged six months or above were suspended earlier. Covid-19 vaccination services under the government programme will resume in the fourth quarter of this year

The Covid-19 vaccination programme for people aged 12 or above will be temporarily suspended after September 5.

美国 FDA 接受 ENFLONSIATM(clesrovimab-cfor)补充生物制品许可申请,以更新其 呼吸道合胞病毒(呼吸道合胞病毒)下呼吸道疾病适应症,纳入 2 岁以下儿童

U.S. FDA Accepts sBLA for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to Update its Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Lower Respiratory Tract Disease Indication to Include Children Under ...
新闻简报美国食品和药物管理局 (FDA) 已接受 ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) 的补充生物制品许可申请 (sBLA),以扩大其适应症,包括预防两岁以下儿童的 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 下呼吸道疾病 (LRTD),这些儿童在第二个 RSV 季节期间患严重 RSV 疾病的风险增加。这一决定得到了 3 期 SMART 试验 (MK-1654-007) 数据的支持,该试验在 RSVVW’26 上发表,并发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》和《JAMA 儿科学》上。FDA 已将 PDUFA 的决定日期定为 2027 年 3 月 22 日。EMA 还于 2026 年 7 月接受了欧盟的申请。ENFLONSIATM 是第一个也是唯一一个无需根据体重给药的婴儿 RSV LRTD 预防选择。它已在美国、加拿大、欧盟、中国和日本等 40 多个国家/地区获得批准,用于在第一个 RSV 季节的婴儿中使用。该药物旨在提供长达五个月(典型 RSV 季节)的保护,并在 RSV 季节出生的婴儿出生后第一周内给药。该试验纳入了 RSV 第 1 季的 502 名婴儿和 RSV 第 2 季的 276 名婴儿,其中 99% 的第 2 季参与者患有慢性肺病或先天性心脏病。监管文件旨在进一步减轻 RSV 对家庭和医疗保健系统的负担。
查看新闻五要素
时间
FDA 于 2026 年 8 月 6 日接受了 sBLA,并将 PDUFA 日期定为 2027 年 3 月 22 日。3 期 SMART 试验结果于 2025 年 9 月和 2026 年 7 月发布。
地点与对象
美国食品药品监督管理局 (FDA) 和欧洲药品管理局 (EMA) 参与其中。目标人群是患有严重 RSV 疾病风险较高的两岁以下儿童,包括患有慢性肺病、先天性心脏病或早期/中度早产的儿童。
事件
The FDA accepted a supplemental Biologics License Application (sBLA) for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to expand its indication to include prevention of RSV lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) in children under two years of age during their second RSV season. This is supported by data from the Phase 3 SMART trial.
规模、影响与风险
ENFLONSIATM 已在 40 多个国家获得批准,可用于婴儿 RSV 的第一个季节。该试验纳入了 RSV 第 1 季的 502 名婴儿和 RSV 第 2 季的 276 名婴儿,其中 99% 的第 2 季参与者患有慢性肺病或先天性心脏病。该药物旨在减轻 RSV 对家庭和医疗保健系统的负担。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA已接受sBLA,EMA已接受欧盟的申请。试验数据支持监管备案,该药物目前已在多个国家获得批准。回应仍在进行中,进一步的监管备案正在进行中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

U.S. FDA Accepts sBLA for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to Update its Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Lower Respiratory Tract Disease Indication to Include Children Under ...

News Brief
The U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has accepted a supplemental Biologics License Application (sBLA) for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to expand its indication to include prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) in children under two years of age at increased risk for severe RSV disease during their second RSV season. This decision is supported by data from the Phase 3 SMART trial (MK-1654-007), which was presented at RSVVW’26 and published in the New England Journal of Medicine and JAMA Pediatrics. The FDA has set a PDUFA date of March 22, 2027, for a decision. The EMA also accepted the application for the European Union in July 2026. ENFLONSIATM is the first and only preventive option for RSV LRTD in infants without weight-based dosing. It is approved in over 40 countries, including the U.S., Canada, the EU, China, and Japan, for use in infants during their first RSV season. The drug is designed to provide protection for up to five months, a typical RSV season, and is administered within the first week of life for infants born during the RSV season. The trial included 502 infants in RSV season 1 and 276 in RSV season 2, with 99% of season 2 participants having chronic lung disease or congenital heart disease. The regulatory filings aim to further reduce the burden of RSV on families and healthcare systems.
Original source text (English)
RAHWAY, N.J.--(BUSINESS WIRE)--Aug 6, 2026-- Merck (NYSE: MRK), known as MSD outside of the United States and Canada, announced today that the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has accepted a supplemental Biologics License Application (sBLA) for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to expand its indication to include prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) in children under two years of age at increased risk for severe RSV disease through their second RSV season. The FDA has set a Prescription Drug User Fee Act (PDUFA), or target action, date of March 22, 2027. In July 2026, the European Medicines Agency (EMA) accepted the application to expand marketing authorization for ENFLONSIA in the European Union (EU) for the same population. ENFLONSIA was approved by the U.S. FDA in June 2025 and by the European Commission in April 2026 for the prevention of RSV LRTD in infants born during or entering their first RSV season. ENFLONSIA is the first and only preventive option against RSV LRTD administered to infants without the need for weight-based dosing. “For some children, the potentially serious impact of RSV does not stop after their first RSV season,” said Dr. Macaya Douoguih, vice president, Therapeutic Area Head, Global Clinical Development, Merck Research Laboratories. “The recent FDA and EMA regulatory filings represent important opportunities to help expand protection with ENFLONSIA for vulnerable children under two years of age during their second RSV season – potentially further reducing the significant burden of RSV on families and health care systems.” Even with protection during their first RSV season, certain children – including those born with chronic lung disease, congenital heart disease or early or moderate preterm birth with certain risk conditions – remain vulnerable to severe RSV disease during their second RSV season. Both the U.S. and EU applications are supported by data from the Phase 3 SMART trial (MK-1654-007), which were presented at RSVVW’26, the 9th conference of the Respiratory Syncytial Virus Foundation (ReSViNET), in February 2026 and published in the New England Journal of Medicine in September 2025 (interim RSV season 1 results) and JAMA Pediatrics in July 2026 (full results). ENFLONSIA is approved in more than 40 countries worldwide, including the United States , Canada, the European Union , China and Japan, for use in infants during their first RSV season. Regulatory filings are underway for approval in additional countries globally, as well as for indication expansion where already approved. About the Phase 3 SMART Trial The SMART trial (MK-1654-007) ( NCT04938830 ) was a Phase 3, randomized, partially-blind, palivizumab-controlled, multicenter study to evaluate the safety, efficacy and pharmacokinetics of ENFLONSIA in infants and children at increased risk for severe RSV disease over two RSV seasons. The trial enrolled early (<29 weeks gestational age [GA]) or moderate preterm infants (≥29 to ≤35 weeks GA) and infants with chronic lung disease (CLD) of prematurity or congenital heart disease (CHD) of any GA. In RSV season 1, participants were randomized 1:1 to receive either a 105 mg dose of ENFLONSIA (n=502) or monthly palivizumab (n=501) by intramuscular (IM) injection. In RSV season 2, eligible participants (children under 2 years of age with CLD, CHD or early or moderate preterm birth with certain risk conditions) received an additional open-label 210 mg dose of ENFLONSIA by IM injection (n=276, of whom 138 received ENFLONSIA and 138 received palivizumab in RSV season 1). Nearly all participants (99%) who received ENFLONSIA in RSV season 2 had CLD (n=229) or CHD (n=43). About RSV Globally Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is a contagious virus that causes widespread seasonal infections and can lead to serious respiratory conditions such as bronchiolitis and pneumonia. As a leading cause of hospitalization among infants globally, there is persisting unmet need for RSV preventive options for both healthy and high-risk infants during their first RSV season as well as for children at increased risk for severe RSV disease through their second RSV season. RSV season is the time of year when RSV infections are most common, usually occurring autumn through spring of the next year in temperate climates. Timing and severity in a given community or region can vary year to year. About ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) in the U.S. ENFLONSIA is Merck’s FDA-approved extended half-life monoclonal antibody (mAb) indicated for passive immunization for the prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lower respiratory tract disease in newborns and infants who are born during or entering their first RSV season. ENFLONSIA is administered using the same dose regardless of weight (105 mg/0.7 mL in a prefilled syringe) and is designed to provide direct, rapid and durable protection through 5 months, a typical RSV season. For infants born during the RSV season, ENFLONSIA is to be administered within the first week of life. For infants born outside of the RSV season, ENFLONSIA should be administered shortly before the RSV season begins. For infants undergoing cardiac surgery with cardiopulmonary bypass during or entering their first RSV season, an additional 105 mg dose is recommended as soon as the infant is stable after surgery. ENFLONSIA has a 30-month shelf life. Selected Safety Information for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) in the U.S. Do not administer ENFLONSIA to infants with a history of serious hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, to any component of ENFLONSIA. Serious hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, have been observed with other human immunoglobulin G1 (IgG1) monoclonal antibodies. If signs or symptoms of a clinically significant hypersensitivity reaction or anaphylaxis occur, initiate appropriate medications and/or supportive therapy. ENFLONSIA may interfere with some immunologically-based RSV diagnostic assays (i.e., rapid antigen tests) as observed in laboratory studies. Confirmation using a reverse transcriptase polymerase chain reaction (RT-PCR) assay is recommended when rapid antigen assay results are negative and clinical observations are consistent with RSV infection. The most common adverse reactions were injection-site erythema (3.8%), injection-site swelling (2.7%) and rash (2.3%). We foster a diverse and inclusive global workforce and operate responsibly every day to enable a safe, sustainable and healthy future for all people and communities. View five news elements
Time
The FDA accepted the sBLA on August 6, 2026, with a PDUFA date set for March 22, 2027. The Phase 3 SMART trial results were published in September 2025 and July 2026.
Location and population
Food and Drug Administration (FDA) and the European Medicines Agency (EMA) are involved. The target population is children under two years of age at increased risk for severe RSV disease, including those with chronic lung disease, congenital heart disease, or early/moderate preterm birth.
Event
The FDA accepted a supplemental Biologics License Application (sBLA) for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to expand its indication to include prevention of RSV lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) in children under two years of age during their second RSV season. This is supported by data from the Phase 3 SMART trial.
Scale, impact and risk
ENFLONSIATM is approved in over 40 countries for use in infants during their first RSV season. The trial included 502 infants in RSV season 1 and 276 in RSV season 2, with 99% of season 2 participants having chronic lung disease or congenital heart disease. The drug is intended to reduce the burden of RSV on families and healthcare systems.
Response, status and uncertainty
The FDA has accepted the sBLA, and the EMA has accepted the application for the EU. The trial data supports the regulatory filings, and the drug is currently approved in multiple countries. The response is ongoing with further regulatory filings in progress.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)

Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV)
新闻简报呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 仍然是一个重大的全球健康威胁,特别是影响婴儿和老年人。婴儿期早期 RSV 感染会增加儿童哮喘的风险,尤其是有过敏或哮喘家族史的儿童。对于老年人来说,RSV 会导致严重的后果和心脏并发症,疫苗 可以有效地将 60 岁及以上成年人的住院率减少 80% 以上。孕产妇 疫苗接种 和 尼塞韦单抗 在新生儿风险最高的月份提供补充保护。世界卫生组织发布了第一份关于婴儿 RSV 免疫的立场文件,强调需要全年获得免疫接种,以尽量减少季节性 暴发。尽管取得了这些进步,但仍需要采取额外的干预措施,因为仅 HEPA 净化器并不能完全减少教室的病毒暴露。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-02。证据中没有报告具体的事件日期。
地点与对象
全球范围,特别关注婴儿、老年人以及早产儿、患有基础疾病的儿童、白血病患者等弱势群体。
事件
呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是五岁以下儿童急性下呼吸道感染的主要原因,对全世界儿童健康构成持续威胁。婴儿早期感染 RSV 会显着增加患儿童哮喘的风险,尤其是有过敏或哮喘家族史的儿童。RSV 还会导致老年人出现严重后果和心脏并发症。
规模、影响与风险
RSV 导致成人住院率较高,丹麦的一项大型真实世界随机试验发现,RSVpreF 疫苗 将 60 岁及以上成人与 RSV 相关的住院率降低了 80% 以上。单剂量的 RSV 疫苗 可以为老年人提供 58% 的住院保护,但对于免疫功能低下的个体来说,效果有所不同。全年接种 RSV 疫苗可能会减少全国主要季节性 暴发 的发生。
应对、状态与不确定性
儿科专家正在为呼吸道病毒季节期间 RSV 病例的预期增加做好准备。孕产妇 疫苗接种 和 尼塞韦单抗 共同提供补充策略,在新生儿风险最高的月份提供保护。世界卫生组织 (WHO) 发布了第一份关于保护婴儿免受 RSV 感染的免疫产品的立场文件。然而,HEPA 净化器并不能完全减少教室病毒暴露,这表明需要采取额外的干预措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV)

News Brief
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) remains a significant global health threat, particularly affecting infants and older adults. Early RSV infection in infancy increases the risk of childhood asthma, especially in children with a family history of allergy or asthma. For older adults, RSV causes severe outcomes and heart complications, with vaccines showing effectiveness in reducing hospitalizations by over 80% in adults aged 60 and over. Maternal vaccination and nirsevimab provide complementary protection for newborns during their highest-risk months. The WHO has issued its first position paper on RSV immunization for infants, emphasizing the need for year-round access to immunization to minimize seasonal outbreaks. Despite these advances, additional interventions are needed as HEPA purifiers alone do not fully reduce classroom viral exposure.
Original source text (English)
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Nirsevimab is a long-acting monoclonal antibody that provides season-long protection against RSV in infants, reducing medically attended infections and hospitalizations. Maternal vaccination and nirsevimab together offer complementary strategies to protect newborns during their highest-risk months. Time
Published on 2026-08-02. No specific event date is reported in the evidence.
Location and population
Global, with specific focus on infants, older adults, and vulnerable populations such as premature babies, children with underlying diseases, and leukemia patients.
Event
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) is a leading cause of acute lower respiratory infections in children under five and poses a persistent threat to child health worldwide. Early-infancy infection with RSV significantly increases the risk of developing childhood asthma, especially in children with a family history of allergy or asthma. RSV also causes severe outcomes and heart complications in older adults.
Scale, impact and risk
RSV leads to high hospitalization rates in adults, with a large real-world randomized trial in Denmark finding that the RSVpreF vaccine reduced RSV-related hospitalizations in adults aged 60 and over by over 80%. A single dose of the RSV vaccine offers 58% protection against hospitalization in older adults, but effectiveness varies in immunocompromised individuals. Year-round access to RSV immunization may reduce major seasonal outbreaks across the nation.
Response, status and uncertainty
Pediatric experts are preparing for an expected rise in cases of RSV during the respiratory virus season. Maternal vaccination and nirsevimab together offer complementary strategies to protect newborns during their highest-risk months. The World Health Organization (WHO) has published its first-ever position paper on immunization products to protect infants against RSV. However, HEPA purifiers do not fully reduce classroom viral exposure, suggesting additional interventions are needed.

FDA Accepts Merck Application to Expand ENFLONSIA Protection Into Second RSV Season - Infection Control Today

News Brief
The FDA has accepted Merck's application to expand the protection provided by ENFLONSIA into the second respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) season. This decision was reported by Infection Control Today on August 10, 2026. The article highlights the importance of infection prevention strategies, including the need for standardized cleaning of reusable lead aprons, which were found to have bacterial contamination in environmental testing. While the article does not report any confirmed cases or deaths related to RSV, it emphasizes the ongoing efforts to improve infection control practices in healthcare settings. The article also touches on broader topics such as HIV treatment advancements and the role of infection preventionists in managing outbreaks. The information is based on a syndicated summary, and the full original article body could not be extracted.

U.S. FDA Accepts sBLA for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to Update its Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Lower Respiratory Tract Disease Indication to Include Children Under Two Years at Increased Risk for Severe RSV for Their Second Season - BioSpace
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 6 日,默克宣布美国 FDA 接受 ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) 的补充生物制剂许可申请,以扩大其 RSV 下呼吸道疾病适应症,将第二季严重 RSV 风险增加的两岁以下儿童纳入其中。该文件得到了第 3 阶段 SMART 试验数据的支持,旨在解决高危婴儿在第一个 RSV 季节之后未得到满足的扩展保护需求。FDA 将处方药使用者付费法案的目标行动日期定为 2027 年 3 月 22 日。欧洲药品管理局于 2026 年 7 月接受了并行申请。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-08-06;美国食品药品监督管理局(FDA)同日宣布接受补充生物制品许可申请(sBLA);处方药用户收费法案(PDUFA)目标行动日期定为2027-03-22。
地点与对象
美国;目标人群:在第二个 RSV 季节期间患严重 RSV 疾病的风险增加的两岁以下儿童。
事件
FDA 接受了 ENFLONSIATM(clesrovimab-cfor)的补充生物制剂许可申请(sBLA),以扩大其适应症,包括在特定高危儿科人群中预防 RSV 下呼吸道疾病(LRTD)。
规模、影响与风险
不适用于监管备案活动;没有病例数、死亡或暴露报告。该申请针对的是易感婴儿在第二个 RSV 季节期间未满足的 RSV 预防需求。
应对、状态与不确定性
Under regulatory review; PDUFA target action date is 2027-03-22. The application is supported by Phase 3 SMART trial data (NCT04938830) presented at RSVVW’26 and published in peer-reviewed journals. EMA accepted a parallel application in July 2026.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

U.S. FDA Accepts sBLA for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to Update its Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Lower Respiratory Tract Disease Indication to Include Children Under Two Years at Increased Risk for Severe RSV for Their Second Season - BioSpace

News Brief
On August 6, 2026, Merck announced that the U.S. FDA accepted a supplemental Biologics License Application for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to expand its RSV lower respiratory tract disease indication to include children under two years at increased risk for severe RSV during their second season. The filing, supported by Phase 3 SMART trial data, targets an unmet need for extended protection in high-risk infants beyond their first RSV season. The FDA set a Prescription Drug User Fee Act target action date of March 22, 2027. A parallel application was accepted by the European Medicines Agency in July 2026.
Original source text (English)
News Drug Development FDA Manufacturing Deals Business Job Trends Cell and Gene Therapy Cancer Opinions Press Releases All News & Releases Podcasts Events Jobs Companies Hotbeds More Reports Career Advice NextGen: Top Startups to Watch 40 Under 40 Best Places to Work Employer Resources Advertise Advertise Talent Solutions Post Jobs Submit a Press Release Submit an Event SUBSCRIBE Menu SUBSCRIBE Show Search News Drug Development FDA Manufacturing Deals Business Job Trends Cell and Gene Therapy Cancer Opinions Press Releases All News & Releases Podcasts Events Jobs Companies Hotbeds More Reports Career Advice NextGen: Top Startups to Watch 40 Under 40 Best Places to Work Employer Resources Advertise Advertise Talent Solutions Post Jobs Submit a Press Release Submit an Event Search Query Submit Search Press Releases U.S. FDA Accepts sBLA for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to Update its Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Lower Respiratory Tract Disease Indication to Include Children Under Two Years at Increased Risk for Severe RSV for Their Second Season August 6, 2026 | 8 min read Twitter LinkedIn Facebook Email Print EMA also accepts application for the same population for ENFLONSIA based on results from the Phase 3 SMART trial If approved, children under two years of age at increased risk for severe RSV disease will be eligible to receive an additional dose of ENFLONSIA for their second RSV season RAHWAY, N.J.--(BUSINESS WIRE)--$MRK #MRK--Merck (NYSE: MRK), known as MSD outside of the United States and Canada, announced today that the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has accepted a supplemental Biologics License Application (sBLA) for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) to expand its indication to include prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) in children under two years of age at increased risk for severe RSV disease through their second RSV season. The FDA has set a Prescription Drug User Fee Act (PDUFA), or target action, date of March 22, 2027. In July 2026, the European Medicines Agency (EMA) accepted the application to expand marketing authorization for ENFLONSIA in the European Union (EU) for the same population. ENFLONSIA was approved by the U.S. FDA in June 2025 and by the European Commission in April 2026 for the prevention of RSV LRTD in infants born during or entering their first RSV season. ENFLONSIA is the first and only preventive option against RSV LRTD administered to infants without the need for weight-based dosing. “For some children, the potentially serious impact of RSV does not stop after their first RSV season,” said Dr. Macaya Douoguih, vice president, Therapeutic Area Head, Global Clinical Development, Merck Research Laboratories. “The recent FDA and EMA regulatory filings represent important opportunities to help expand protection with ENFLONSIA for vulnerable children under two years of age during their second RSV season – potentially further reducing the significant burden of RSV on families and health care systems.” Even with protection during their first RSV season, certain children – including those born with chronic lung disease, congenital heart disease or early or moderate preterm birth with certain risk conditions – remain vulnerable to severe RSV disease during their second RSV season. Both the U.S. and EU applications are supported by data from the Phase 3 SMART trial (MK-1654-007), which were presented at RSVVW’26, the 9th conference of the Respiratory Syncytial Virus Foundation (ReSViNET), in February 2026 and published in the New England Journal of Medicine in September 2025 (interim RSV season 1 results) and JAMA Pediatrics in July 2026 (full results). ENFLONSIA is approved in more than 40 countries worldwide, including the United States, Canada, the European Union, China and Japan, for use in infants during their first RSV season. Regulatory filings are underway for approval in additional countries globally, as well as for indication expansion where already approved. About the Phase 3 SMART Trial The SMART trial (MK-1654-007) (NCT04938830) was a Phase 3, randomized, partially-blind, palivizumab-controlled, multicenter study to evaluate the safety, efficacy and pharmacokinetics of ENFLONSIA in infants and children at increased risk for severe RSV disease over two RSV seasons. The trial enrolled early (<29 weeks gestational age [GA]) or moderate preterm infants (≥29 to ≤35 weeks GA) and infants with chronic lung disease (CLD) of prematurity or congenital heart disease (CHD) of any GA. In RSV season 1, participants were randomized 1:1 to receive either a 105 mg dose of ENFLONSIA (n=502) or monthly palivizumab (n=501) by intramuscular (IM) injection. In RSV season 2, eligible participants (children under 2 years of age with CLD, CHD or early or moderate preterm birth with certain risk conditions) received an additional open-label 210 mg dose of ENFLONSIA by IM injection (n=276, of whom 138 received ENFLONSIA and 138 received palivizumab in RSV season 1). Nearly all participants (99%) who received ENFLONSIA in RSV season 2 had CLD (n=229) or CHD (n=43). About RSV Globally Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is a contagious virus that causes widespread seasonal infections and can lead to serious respiratory conditions such as bronchiolitis and pneumonia. As a leading cause of hospitalization among infants globally, there is persisting unmet need for RSV preventive options for both healthy and high-risk infants during their first RSV season as well as for children at increased risk for severe RSV disease through their second RSV season. RSV season is the time of year when RSV infections are most common, usually occurring autumn through spring of the next year in temperate climates. Timing and severity in a given community or region can vary year to year. About ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) in the U.S. ENFLONSIA is Merck’s FDA-approved extended half-life monoclonal antibody (mAb) indicated for passive immunization for the prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lower respiratory tract disease in newborns and infants who are born during or entering their first RSV season. ENFLONSIA is administered using the same dose regardless of weight (105 mg/0.7 mL in a prefilled syringe) and is designed to provide direct, rapid and durable protection through 5 months, a typical RSV season. For infants born during the RSV season, ENFLONSIA is to be administered within the first week of life. For infants born outside of the RSV season, ENFLONSIA should be administered shortly before the RSV season begins. For infants undergoing cardiac surgery with cardiopulmonary bypass during or entering their first RSV season, an additional 105 mg dose is recommended as soon as the infant is stable after surgery. ENFLONSIA has a 30-month shelf life. Selected Safety Information for ENFLONSIATM (clesrovimab-cfor) in the U.S. Do not administer ENFLONSIA to infants with a history of serious hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, to any component of ENFLONSIA. Serious hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, have been observed with other human immunoglobulin G1 (IgG1) monoclonal antibodies. If signs or symptoms of a clinically significant hypersensitivity reaction or anaphylaxis occur, initiate appropriate medications and/or supportive therapy. ENFLONSIA may interfere with some immunologically-based RSV diagnostic assays (i.e., rapid antigen tests) as observed in laboratory studies. Confirmation using a reverse transcriptase polymerase chain reaction (RT-PCR) assay is recommended when rapid antigen assay results are negative and clinical observations are consistent with RSV infection. The most common adverse reactions were injection-site erythema (3.8%), injection-site swelling (2.7%) and rash (2.3%). B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rsv season approaches as doctors urge infant protection

News Brief
As fall approaches, U.S. physicians are urging heightened protection for infants against Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV), the leading cause of pneumonia and airway inflammation in newborns. Dr. David Janese, a family medicine physician with Ochsner LSU Health in Shreveport, Louisiana, highlights new preventive options, including vaccinations, and advises parents to consult their child’s pediatrician. Experts emphasize keeping infants’ environments germ-free and seeking immediate medical attention if symptoms such as fever, breathing difficulties, or lethargy arise. While RSV season looms, no specific case counts or outbreaks are detailed in the guidance, which focuses on proactive measures to shield the most vulnerable.
Original source text (English)
SHREVEPORT, La. ( KTAL/KMSS ) — Fall is quickly approaching, which means respiratory virus season is right around the corner. Doctors say infants are the most susceptible to respiratory viruses. RSV, or Respiratory Syncytial Virus, is the most common virus that puts newborns at risk for pneumonia and airway inflammation. Shreveport doctor urges UV safety during heat rise Experts say the easiest way for parents to protect their little ones is to keep their environment as germ-free as possible. With recent advancements in medicine, physicians say it is best to talk with your child's medical provider to see if vaccinations are best for them. "So, for the first time, families have access to multiple ways to actually build a shield around their newborn before the RSV actually reaches that baby," says Dr. David Janese, family medicine physician with Ochsner LSU Health. PCOS now PMOS: New name reflects broader health impact "So make sure that you stay informed, you're asking questions, you're doing your own research, because when it comes down to it, you're the parent. You're the protector of your child. You have to do what you want to do, and we want to do what's best for the baby," Janese says. Dr. Janese says if your baby has a fever, trouble breathing, and is less responsive, contact your child's pediatrician. Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. All rights reserved. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

尼塞韦单抗 前两年的免疫结果 - Bioengineer.org

Nirsevimab Immunization Outcomes in the First Two Years - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报发表在《JAMA Pediatrics》上的一项研究报告称,尼塞韦单抗(一种长效单克隆抗体)在给药后的第一年显着减少了婴儿因 RSV 相关下呼吸道感染而住院的情况。在 2023-2024 年和 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节,保护效果是一致的,与未接受治疗的同龄人相比,接受治疗的儿童入院人数大幅减少。该益处是针对 RSV 的,并不适用于非 RSV 感染或哮喘相关的住院治疗。保护作用在第二年减弱,与抗体的设计作用持续时间一致。研究人员强调,虽然研究结果支持现实世界的有效性,但该研究的观察设计限制了因果推断,并且未测量的因素可能会影响结果。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-10;研究涵盖 2023-2024 年和 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节,并在给药后的头两年内评估结果。
地点与对象
RSV 季节性传播环境中的婴儿和幼儿,特别是早产儿或患有基础疾病的婴儿和幼儿。
事件
发表在《JAMA Pediatrics》上的一项研究报告称,尼塞韦单抗(一种长效单克隆抗体)在给药后第一年可大幅减少因 RSV 相关下呼吸道感染而住院的人数。
规模、影响与风险
该研究观察到,在连续两个 RSV 季节的随访第一年中,与未接受 尼塞韦单抗 治疗的儿童相比,接受 尼塞韦单抗 治疗的儿童因 RSV 相关住院的情况要少得多。未观察到因非 RSV 感染或哮喘而住院的人数减少,这表明存在病原体特异性效应。该福利不会延续到第二年。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是观察性的,表明存在关联而不是确定的因果关系。治疗 决策、基本健康状况、出生史、医疗保健获取和其他特征的差异可能会影响结果。保护作用仅限于第一年,年龄较大的儿童面临的风险较低。研究结果通过显示多个季节的真实表现来补充临床试验证据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nirsevimab Immunization Outcomes in the First Two Years - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published in JAMA Pediatrics reports that nirsevimab, a long-acting monoclonal antibody, significantly reduced hospitalizations from RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections in infants during the first year after administration. The protective effect was consistent across the 2023–2024 and 2024–2025 RSV seasons, with substantially fewer admissions among treated children compared to untreated peers. The benefit was specific to RSV and did not extend to non-RSV infections or asthma-related hospitalizations. Protection waned by the second year, aligning with the antibody’s designed duration of action. Researchers emphasize that while the findings support real-world effectiveness, the study’s observational design limits causal inference and unmeasured factors may influence outcomes.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Tuesday, August 11, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Nirsevimab Immunization Outcomes in the First Two Years by Bioengineer August 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Nirsevimab, a long-acting monoclonal antibody designed to protect infants from respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), was associated with a substantial reduction in hospitalizations caused by RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections during the first year after administration, according to a study published in JAMA Pediatrics. The findings were observed across both the 2023–2024 and 2024–2025 RSV seasons, providing early real-world evidence that the preventive treatment can reduce severe disease requiring hospital care in young children. RSV is one of the most common causes of lower respiratory tract infection in infants and young children. Although infection often produces cold-like symptoms, the virus can spread into the small airways and lungs, causing bronchiolitis or pneumonia. Infants, particularly those born prematurely or with underlying medical conditions, are at increased risk of respiratory distress, dehydration, and hospitalization. Because the virus circulates seasonally and can infect children repeatedly throughout life, reducing the severity of a first infection is a major public health objective. Discover more Drugs & Medications Biological Sciences Health Pharmaceuticals & Biotech Nirsevimab works by providing passive immunity rather than stimulating the infant’s immune system to produce its own antibodies. The drug is a laboratory-engineered antibody that binds to the fusion protein on the surface of RSV. This protein is essential for the virus to enter human cells and spread between them. By attaching to a vulnerable region of the fusion protein, nirsevimab can neutralize RSV particles before they establish infection or limit the progression of infection after exposure. Its extended duration of action allows a single administration to provide protection throughout much of an RSV season. The study evaluated outcomes during two consecutive RSV seasons and compared hospitalization patterns among children who received nirsevimab with those who did not. Its principal focus was hospitalization associated with RSV lower respiratory tract infection, a clinically important endpoint because it reflects severe disease rather than infection detected only through outpatient testing. The investigators also examined whether nirsevimab was associated with changes in hospitalizations from non-RSV infections and asthma, conditions that could help indicate whether the observed effect was specifically linked to RSV prevention. Across the 2023–2024 and 2024–2025 seasons, children who received nirsevimab experienced substantially fewer hospitalizations related to RSV lower respiratory tract infections during the first year of follow-up. The consistency of the association across both seasons is notable because the timing and intensity of RSV circulation can vary from year to year. Seasonal differences in viral transmission, population immunity, healthcare-seeking behavior, and the prevalence of other respiratory pathogens can all influence hospitalization rates. Observing a similar protective pattern in two successive seasons strengthens the evidence that the reduction was related to nirsevimab rather than to a single unusual epidemic. The researchers did not find evidence that nirsevimab reduced hospitalizations caused by non-RSV infections. This distinction is biologically important. Nirsevimab targets RSV specifically and does not function as a broad antiviral or general immune stimulant. A selective reduction in RSV-related admissions, without a comparable decline in hospitalizations from unrelated infections, supports the interpretation that the treatment’s benefit is pathogen-specific. It also suggests that the results were not simply the consequence of broader differences in healthcare access, general illness prevention, or the likelihood that treated children were hospitalized. The analysis also found no association between nirsevimab and reduced asthma-related hospitalizations. Asthma is influenced by multiple factors, including genetic susceptibility, airway inflammation, environmental exposures, and viral triggers. Severe RSV infection early in life has been linked in some research with later respiratory problems, but a potential long-term relationship cannot be established by observing short-term hospitalization patterns alone. The absence of a detected reduction in asthma admissions indicates that the immediate preventive effect of nirsevimab should not be interpreted as evidence that the antibody prevents asthma or modifies all forms of respiratory disease. Discover more Medical Literature & Resources infection Respiratory Conditions respiratory respiratory disease The apparent benefit was limited to the first year of follow-up. During the second year, the study did not identify a corresponding reduction in RSV-related hospitalizations. Several explanations are possible. The antibody’s protection is designed to last through a defined period rather than indefinitely, and children may encounter RSV after the period of effective antibody coverage has ended. In addition, older children generally face a different risk profile than infants, with more mature airways and immune systems reducing the likelihood of severe disease. Changes in exposure, vaccination or prophylaxis practices, and the underlying circulation of RSV may also contribute to differences between the first and second years. Because the study was observational, the findings demonstrate an association rather than definitive proof of causation. Treatment decisions, underlying health, birth history, healthcare access, and other characteristics may differ between children who receive nirsevimab and those who do not. Researchers use statistical methods to account for measurable differences, but unmeasured factors can remain. Even so, the results complement clinical trial evidence by showing how nirsevimab performed under routine conditions and across more than one RSV season. The findings suggest that targeted antibody protection can meaningfully reduce severe RSV disease in infants during the period when they are most vulnerable, while underscoring that it does not prevent unrelated infections or guarantee protection beyond the first year. Subject of Research: Nirsevimab and its association with RSV lower respiratory tract infection hospitalizations in children. 问答:哪些患有 RTI 的住院儿童患严重疾病的风险最高? - 医师周刊

Q&A: Which Hospitalized Children With RTIs Face the Highest Risk for Severe Disease? - Physician's Weekly
新闻简报队列研究 对 2022 年 7 月至 2023 年 6 月期间因急性呼吸道感染 (RTI) 住院的 2,585 名加拿大儿童进行的回顾性研究发现,超过五分之一的儿童出现严重疾病,其中 21.3% 需要强化干预,6.7% 的严重病例最终死亡。一岁以下的婴儿、十岁以上的青少年、从另一家医院转院的儿童以及患有多种慢性病(尤其是神经系统、发育或遗传疾病)的患者的风险最高。呼吸道合胞病毒 和肠道病毒-鼻病毒是最常检测到的病原体,但病毒合并感染并没有增加严重程度。专家建议对高危患者进行更密切的监测并尽早升级,并在有需要时进行常规免疫和针对 RSV 的预防。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-07;学习期限2022年7月至2023年6月。
地点与对象
两个加拿大儿科中心:病童医院(安大略省多伦多)和 BC 儿童医院(不列颠哥伦比亚省温哥华);2,585 名因急性呼吸道感染 (RTI) 住院的儿童,中位年龄 2.5 岁。
事件
队列研究 回顾性研究报告称,超过五分之一的住院儿童患有严重疾病;从转诊医院转院的儿童、患有多种慢性病的儿童、1 岁以下的婴儿和 10 岁以上的青少年中观察到的风险最高。研究结果发表在《JAMA Network Open》上,并由外部专家进行了讨论。
规模、影响与风险
2,585名入学儿童;551 名(21.3%)符合严重疾病标准,包括心脏骤停、需要无创或有创通气、ECMO 或肾脏替代治疗;37人(重症病例的6.7%)死亡。慢性疾病(尤其是神经系统疾病、发育疾病或遗传疾病)和转移状态与风险增加相关;病毒合并感染(约 15%)与严重程度无关。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究作者建议针对病情复杂的儿童进行有针对性的预防、持续的 监测 以及优先考虑 RSV 预防策略。研究结果强调了患者特定的危险因素而不是单独的病毒 诊断,并强调了解释高度敏感的分子测试的挑战。报告中没有描述积极的公共卫生应对行动。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Q&A: Which Hospitalized Children With RTIs Face the Highest Risk for Severe Disease? - Physician's Weekly

News Brief
A retrospective cohort study of 2,585 Canadian children hospitalized with acute respiratory tract infections (RTIs) between July 2022 and June 2023 found that more than one in five developed severe disease, with 21.3% requiring intensive interventions and 6.7% of severe cases ending in death. Risk was highest among infants under one year, adolescents over ten years, children transferred from another hospital, and those with multiple chronic conditions—particularly neurologic, developmental, or genetic disorders. Respiratory syncytial virus and enterovirus-rhinovirus were the most commonly detected pathogens, but viral coinfections did not increase severity. Experts recommend closer monitoring and earlier escalation for high-risk patients, alongside routine immunizations and RSV-targeted prevention where indicated.
Original source text (English)
Please enable Javascript Skip to main content Contribute to PW Subscribe Specialties Allergy & Immunology Cardiology Critical Care Dermatology Endocrinology Gastroenterology Infectious Disease Nephrology Neurology OB/GYN Oncology / Hematology Ophthalmology Pain Pediatrics Primary Care Psychiatry Pulmonology Rheumatology Surgery Urology Conference Coverage Knowledge Hub Cases and Quizzes Commentary Cartoons Doctor’s Voice Peer-to-Peer Podcast Business of Medicine Home > Critical Care > Q&A: Which Hospitalized Children With RTIs Face the Highest Severe Disease Risk? Q&A: Which Hospitalized Children With RTIs Face the Highest Severe Disease Risk? By Lorraine L. Janeczko, MPH - Last Updated: August 7, 2026 Maria Deza Leon, MD More than one in five children hospitalized with acute respiratory tract infections (RTIs) develop severe disease, and children transferred from a referring hospital face the highest risk, according to a retrospective study conducted at two Canadian pediatric centers. “In this cohort study of hospitalized children with acute RTIs, many developed severe illness requiring intensive interventions. Chronic conditions were associated with increased risk, whereas viral coinfections were common but not associated with disease severity,” lead author Haifa Mtaweh, MD, PhD, of The Hospital for Sick Children in Toronto, Ontario, and her coauthors wrote in JAMA Network Open. “Despite low mortality, the complication burden remained high, underscoring the need for targeted prevention and ongoing surveillance.” Risk Factors for Severe Disease The 2,585 enrolled children (median age, 2.5 years) were hospitalized with acute RTIs between July 2022 and June 2023 at The Hospital for Sick Children and BC Children’s Hospital in Vancouver, British Columbia. Roughly half (50.6%) had at least one chronic condition, with neurologic, developmental, or genetic diagnoses most common. Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and enterovirus-rhinovirus were the predominant pathogens identified, at 30.4% and 25.6%, respectively. Severe illness, including cardiac arrest, the need for noninvasive or invasive ventilation, extracorporeal membrane oxygenation (ECMO), or kidney replacement therapy, occurred among 551 children (21.3%), and of these, 37 (6.7%) died. In multivariable analysis, children transferred from a referring hospital (adjusted risk ratio [aRR], 4.73; 95% CI, 4.01-5.59), children with a single identified virus compared with none (aRR, 1.65; 95% CI, 1.30-2.08), children with two or more chronic conditions compared with none (aRR, 1.62; 95% CI, 1.36-1.93), and patients aged less than 1 year and more than 10 years were at increased risk for severe outcomes. Viral coinfections, detected among approximately 15% of patients, were not associated with severity. An Expert Shares Insights Maria Deza Leon, MD, a pediatric infectious disease physician at Children’s Mercy in Kansas City, Missouri, who was not involved in the study, spoke with Physician’s Weekly about the study and its implications for pediatric acute RTI care. Physician’s Weekly: Which complications should clinicians anticipate among children hospitalized with RTIs, and do these risks vary by viral pathogen? Dr. Deza Leon: Common severe complications included acute respiratory distress syndrome, cardiac complications, septic shock, pneumothorax, and empyema, with some children requiring ECMO or kidney replacement therapy. Although complication rates were generally similar across viruses, influenza was more often associated with ARDS and septic shock, whereas human metapneumovirus was more often associated with cardiac complications and extracorporeal support. These findings suggest that viral identification may help inform risk, but clinical management should remain focused on the patient’s overall trajectory. Which chronic conditions place patients at the highest risk for severe outcomes, and how can clinicians use this information to guide individualized care strategies? Children with neurologic, developmental, and genetic disorders carried the greatest risk for severe disease, with chronic pulmonary and cardiac conditions also associated with increased risk. Risk was highest among children with multiple chronic conditions, highlighting the impact of medical complexity on patient outcomes. Clinicians can use this information to identify high-risk patients early and guide management decisions. Children with complex medical conditions may benefit from closer monitoring, a lower threshold for escalation of care, and careful discharge planning. These findings also support prioritizing preventive measures, including routine immunizations, RSV prevention strategies when appropriate, and timely access to specialty care for medically complex children. What factors might explain why the highest risk concentrates in two groups—infants and older children/adolescents? This elevated risk among infants is not unexpected. Smaller airways, reduced physiologic reserve, and immature immune function make young children especially vulnerable to respiratory infections. The increased risk observed among older children and adolescents is particularly noteworthy. Although hospitalization for respiratory tract infections is less common in these age groups than in infancy, those requiring admission may represent a more medically complex population with underlying comorbidities or more severe illness at presentation. These findings suggest that while the overall burden of respiratory disease is highest in infancy, the potential severity of respiratory tract infections in hospitalized adolescents should not be overlooked. How does coinfection influence disease course and outcomes for these patients? One of the more reassuring findings was that viral coinfection did not appear to increase the risk of severe disease. Coinfections were relatively common, occurring in about 15% of cases, but their frequency was similar among children with severe and nonsevere illness. These findings suggest that detecting multiple respiratory viruses should not automatically be considered a predictor of worse outcomes. The study also highlights the challenges of interpreting highly sensitive molecular testing, which can detect residual viral nucleic acid that may not reflect active infection or meaningfully contribute to disease severity. What key insights does this study offer about identifying high‐risk patients, anticipating complications, and optimizing early supportive care? This study serves as an important reminder that severe respiratory infection remains a significant source of pediatric morbidity, even in the post-pandemic era. More than one in five hospitalized children met criteria for severe disease, and nearly one-quarter required pediatric ICU admission. The findings reinforce the importance of focusing on patient-specific risk factors rather than the viral diagnosis alone, with particular attention to children with medical complexity and multiple chronic conditions. Transfer from an outside hospital may also serve as a useful marker of greater illness severity. In addition, the results support continued use of preventive strategies, including vaccination and respiratory syncytial virus-targeted prevention when indicated. Clinicians should also remain alert for nonrespiratory complications, such as cardiac dysfunction and septic shock, particularly in children whose clinical status is worsening. Overall, although mortality was uncommon, the burden of severe illness, intensive care utilization, and associated complications remained substantial, underscoring the importance of early recognition, close monitoring, and aggressive supportive care. Contributor Maria Deza Leon, MD Pediatric Infectious Disease Physician Director, Vaccines for Children Program Children’s Mercy Disclosure: Maria Deza Leon, MD, has indicated to Physician’s Weekly that she has no financial interests to disclose. Any views and opinions expressed are those of the author(s) and/or participants and do not necessarily reflect the views, policy, or position of Physician’s Weekly, their employees, and affiliates. Time
Published 2026-08-07; study period July 2022–June 2023.
Location and population
Two Canadian pediatric centers: The Hospital for Sick Children (Toronto, Ontario) and BC Children’s Hospital (Vancouver, British Columbia); 2,585 hospitalized children with acute respiratory tract infections (RTIs), median age 2.5 years.
Event
Retrospective cohort study reporting that more than one in five hospitalized children developed severe disease; highest risk observed in children transferred from a referring hospital, those with multiple chronic conditions, infants under 1 year, and adolescents over 10 years. Findings published in JAMA Network Open and discussed by an external expert.
Scale, impact and risk
2,585 enrolled children; 551 (21.3%) met severe illness criteria including cardiac arrest, need for noninvasive or invasive ventilation, ECMO, or kidney replacement therapy; 37 (6.7% of severe cases) died. Chronic conditions (especially neurologic, developmental, or genetic) and transfer status were associated with increased risk; viral coinfections (≈15%) were not associated with severity.
Response, status and uncertainty
Study authors recommend targeted prevention, ongoing surveillance, and prioritizing RSV prevention strategies for medically complex children. Findings emphasize patient-specific risk factors over viral diagnosis alone and highlight challenges in interpreting highly sensitive molecular tests. No active public-health response actions are described in the report.
